| M1-05/26 | 2026-10-12 | The kernel port `clock.Clock` keeps its name although it has one method; SonarQube Cloud's rule for single-method interfaces is suppressed on that line with `NOSONAR`. | Effective Go names one-method interfaces by the method plus `-er` as a convention, not a rule; `Nower` says less than `Clock`, which names what the port is and is the name its callers (`kernel/ids`) use. Source: [Effective Go, Interface names](https://go.dev/doc/effective_go#interface-names), retrieved 2026-10-12. |
| M1-05/27 | 2026-10-11 | `make arch` passes editor lock files: symbolic links named `.#*` whose target does not exist (`find . -type l \( ! -name '.#*' -o -exec test -e {} \; \) -print`). Every other link still fails, also a `.#` link with an existing target and a dangling link with another name; `test_arch_passes_editor_lock_files_only` and `test_make_arch_passes_an_editor_lock_file` hold this. Because go-arch-lint 1.19.0 aborts at every dangling link below a component with `**` (`failed to resolve path '.../cmd/**'`) before `exclude` or `excludeFiles` apply, `make arch` checks, when there are lock files, a copy of the module without names `.#*` and maps the paths of the copy in its output back to the module; without lock files go-arch-lint runs in the module as before. `test_make_arch_with_editor_lock_files_and_the_real_go_arch_lint` checks this with the pinned go-arch-lint, clean and with a violation. | Minor 1 of the second red team: Emacs creates, for a file it edits, the dangling link `.#<file>` with the target `<user>@<host>.<pid>:<time>` ("The name is constructed by prepending .# to the file name of the buffer", target `<user>@<host>.<pid>:<boot>`, [GNU Emacs Lisp manual, File Locks](https://www.gnu.org/software/emacs/manual/html_node/elisp/File-Locks.html), retrieved 2026-10-12), and `make arch` failed on it locally. The compiler cannot build anything behind a link without a target; a `.#` link that resolves stays forbidden, because like every link it would bring code past go-arch-lint. The closing review and the re-review found that go-arch-lint then failed at the lock file of a Go file, and under `cmd/**` or `internal/wire/**` at every lock file; an entry in `excludeFiles` helped only outside `**`. The go tool skips files whose name starts with a dot ([cmd/go, file system patterns](https://pkg.go.dev/cmd/go#hdr-File_system_patterns): `...` excludes "files and directories with names beginning with "_" or ".""; `go test` ignores such files as well; retrieved 2026-10-12), so the copy hides no code. The abort of go-arch-lint was reproduced with the pinned binary (the test above). |
| M1-05/28 | 2026-10-11 | `kernel-core` keeps its entry `kernel-core: [kernel-core]`, although go-arch-lint already allows it to import its own packages through `commonComponents`; the reason in 02 section 7.2, in the generator and in M1-05/14 is corrected. `TestArchLintSelfEntry` shows on the layout from 02 with the pinned go-arch-lint that `kernel-core` passes without the entry and `chat-domain` fails without its own. | Minor 2 of the second red team: "only with a self entry" was not true for `kernel-core`. The entry stays because it changes no behaviour, keeps the uniform rule "every rule starts with its component", and dropping it would change the generated `.go-arch-lint.yml`, which parallel branches regenerate. Source: go-arch-lint v1.19.0 adds the `commonComponents` to the allowed imports of every component ([allowed_project_imports.go](https://github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint/blob/v1.19.0/internal/services/spec/assembler/allowed_project_imports.go#L29-L33), retrieved 2026-10-12). |
| M1-05/29 | 2026-10-11 | `errs.Error` has `MarshalJSON` with a value receiver: JSON holds the exported fields under their names, with the code from `PublicCode()` (`internal_error` for a code without a kind); `Cause` and the wrapped error are still left out, and a nil `*Error` becomes `null`. `TestJSONWritesPublicCode` holds this for `*Error`, for `Error` values and for both behind an interface. | Minor 3 of the second red team: `json.Marshal(&Error{Code: "bogus"})` wrote `"Code":"bogus"`, while `Error()` and `PublicCode()` return `internal_error` (M1-05/20). The value receiver also reaches an `Error` value behind an interface, which a pointer receiver does not reach; for a nil `*Error`, `encoding/json` writes `null` without calling the method ([encoding/json Marshal](https://pkg.go.dev/encoding/json#Marshal), retrieved 2026-10-12). Responses are still written by the problem writer from M1-18 from `PublicCode` and `Kind`. |
| M1-05/30 | 2026-10-11 | A plain scalar in the register (without quotes and without a tag) that yaml.v3 reads as a string must also be a string for a YAML 1.1 reader, as a key and as a value; otherwise `tools/archgen` rejects it with line, column and YAML path and advises quotes. The rule `yaml11Typed` follows the implicit resolvers of PyYAML 6.0.3 (booleans, numbers with `_` and sexagesimal parts such as `1:20`, timestamps with a time zone after white space, `=`, null, the merge key; the `add_implicit_resolver` calls in [PyYAML 6.0.3 lib/yaml/resolver.py](https://github.com/yaml/pyyaml/blob/6.0.3/lib/yaml/resolver.py), retrieved 2026-10-12) and also rejects `y`, `n`, `yes`, `no`, `on` and `off` in any case. With quotes or `!!str` the value is a string for every reader; only quotes behind the non-specific tag `!` (`! 'no'`) are resolved by PyYAML like a plain scalar, and yaml.v3 keeps no trace of that tag, so the check does not see it. The number `version` must be written in decimal, because yaml.v3 reads `0o1` as 1 and PyYAML as a string. `TestYAML11Rule` and the new cases in `TestRegisterRejects` hold this. | Minor 4 of the second red team: yaml.v3 reads the register as YAML 1.2, PyYAML (M1-06) as YAML 1.1, and `no` was a module name here and `False` there. Second instance of the class from M1-05/16 (two readers see different names), so it is fixed in one place for all resolvers instead of only for the words of the finding. A comparison against PyYAML 6.0.3 over all short number forms and all spellings of the boolean words found no scalar that archgen accepts and PyYAML reads differently; the rule is stricter than PyYAML only for `y` and `n`, which the YAML 1.1 type repository lists as booleans ([bool type](https://yaml.org/type/bool.html), retrieved 2026-10-12), and for mixed spellings, which the finding asks for. |
| M1-05/31 | 2026-10-12 | Ported onto the English `main`: the rulings 25 to 28 of the German follow-up ticket are numbered M1-05/27 to M1-05/30, because main's M1-05/25 (port note) and M1-05/26 (`clock.Clock`) came first; every reference in code, tests and chapter 02 uses the new numbers. | Ruling numbers are stable references, so the numbers already on main stay. |

### M1-07 Configuration schema and loader

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-07/1 | 2026-10-11 | Grammar of a duration: exactly `(0\|[1-9][0-9]*)(ms\|s\|m\|h\|d)`, that is a number without sign, fraction and leading zero and exactly one unit, `d` = 24 h; an overflow of int64 nanoseconds is an error. The canonical form (output of `bothy config print`) uses the largest unit that divides the value without remainder (`120s` as `2m`, zero as `0s`). | One spelling per unit reads without arithmetic for an operator; the chapters name retention periods in days (`30d`), which `time.ParseDuration` does not know, and its fractions and compositions (`1.5h`, `1h30m`) would allow several texts for the same value ([time.ParseDuration](https://pkg.go.dev/time#ParseDuration): units `ns` to `h`, optional fractions, retrieved 2026-10-12). |
| M1-07/2 | 2026-10-11 | The zero value of `config.Kind` is no kind; `NewSchema` rejects a key without a kind. | A forgotten field must not silently mean `string`. |
| M1-07/3 | 2026-10-11 | Every value from file, environment and flag is checked, even if a higher source overrides it. A required key needs a non-empty value. | A wrong value in the file must not surface only when the overriding variable goes away; an empty value does not satisfy a requirement. |
| M1-07/4 | 2026-10-11 | The file is exactly one YAML document. Anchors, aliases, merge keys and explicit tags are errors, as is a scalar or `null` where a section stands; an empty document sets nothing. Syntax errors appear with the text of the YAML parser, except its texts about anchors, which quote the anchor name. | Anchors and merge keys would let one text stand for several keys, and a tag (`!!binary`) would make the text mean something other than what the parser of the kind sees. The other error texts of `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3` are fixed texts with a line number and quote no input (read in the v3.0.5 source of its [scanner](https://github.com/yaml/go-yaml/blob/v3.0.5/scannerc.go) and [parser](https://github.com/yaml/go-yaml/blob/v3.0.5/parserc.go) on 2026-10-11, an own observation, retrieved 2026-10-12; the loader tests hold the texts this ticket relies on). |
| M1-07/5 | 2026-10-11 | A `BOTHY_` variable that appears twice in `Sources.Env` is an error; `BOTHY_CONFIG_FILE` set but empty means no file. | Which entry wins would depend on the caller. An empty variable comes from `${X:-}` in Compose and means "no file". |
| M1-07/6 | 2026-10-11 | `platform/config` knows `server.role` -> `--role` as its only flag; any other key in `Sources.Flags` is a problem. | 02 "Configuration loading" item 2 allows only `--role` and `--config` on the command line; this way the source of a value names the real flag name. |
| M1-07/7 | 2026-10-11 | Canonical text: numbers in base ten, `float` in the shortest form (`strconv.FormatFloat` with `'g'` and precision -1, which uses the smallest number of digits that reads back to the same value, [strconv.FormatFloat](https://pkg.go.dev/strconv#FormatFloat), retrieved 2026-10-12), lists separated by `, ` or as a JSON array if an element has a comma or whitespace at the edge or the first one starts with `[`, prefixes and listen addresses in the normal form of `net/netip`, cron fields with one space, all other kinds unchanged. `bothy config print` puts text with non-printable characters, whitespace at the edge or a quotation mark at the start in Go quotation marks. | The output can be read in again and gives the same value; a line break in a value cannot fake a line of its own. |
| M1-07/8 | 2026-10-11 | A cross check runs only if none of its keys has a problem in any source, and it sees only its keys. Its problem carries the keys joined by `, `, the message `<check>: <error>` and per key `<key> from <source>`. | A cross check on an invalid value would only report a follow-up error; access to an undeclared key is a programming error and ends in a panic in the test. |
| M1-07/9 | 2026-10-11 | `main` reads `os.Environ()` once and passes it through `run` and `dispatch` to every command; for this, the signature of the command table from M1-05 has the parameter `env`. | 02 "Configuration loading" item 5: only `cmd/bothy` reads the environment, once; tests pass their environment explicitly. |
| M1-07/10 | 2026-10-11 | With type resolution, forbidigo also forbids `syscall.Environ`, `(*exec.Cmd).Environ` and `http.ProxyFromEnvironment`, in addition to the functions of the brief. Besides `cmd/bothy/` there is a second exception: test files under `tools/` (`^tools/.+_test\.go$`). `linters.exclusions.generated` is set to `disable`: files with the header line of generated code (`// Code generated ... DO NOT EDIT.`) are checked too; a ticket that introduces generated Go code exempts for its paths (`*.gen.go`, `sqlcgen/`) only the linters the code cannot satisfy, never forbidigo. | `syscall.Environ` reads the environment like `os.Environ`, `Cmd.Environ` returns all of it when `Cmd.Env` is empty, and `ProxyFromEnvironment` routes outgoing calls through `HTTP_PROXY` (finding of the final review; a throwaway file proves each rule). With the default `lax` (and also with `strict`), golangci-lint 2.14 skipped a hand-written file with this header line entirely ([configuration file reference](https://golangci-lint.run/docs/configuration/file/), `linters.exclusions.generated`, retrieved 2026-10-12); with `disable`, forbidigo reports `os.Getenv` there (finding of the re-review, proven on a throwaway file). The module has no generated Go code yet, so the setting costs nothing today. ### M1-22 Egress client and SSRF lint

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-22/1 | 2026-10-11 | The client writes the counter `bothy.egress.blocked` through the interface `egress.Metrics` (`Blocked(ctx, Reason)`) in a fourth field `Deps.Metrics`; without `Metrics` it counts nothing. The allow list must stay equal to 5.3, so the collector file changes with it. |
| M1-22/3 | 2026-10-11 | `TestSECT09_RedirectToPrivateRefused` checks two targets: `https://internal.example.com:<port>/` (allowed only by the policy, resolves to `127.0.0.1`) ends with `address_blocked`, `http://internal.example.com:<port>/` with `redirect_not_allowed`; the target counts 0 requests in all cases. | The brief describes the redirect to `http://` with `address_blocked`; by design point 2, `http` without an entry with `allow_private` already fails before the connection. This way both paths are covered. |
| M1-22/4 | 2026-10-11 | The entries of `egress.allowed_hosts` are checked by the cross check `egress_allowed_hosts` in `internal/wire` with `egress.ParseRule`, not by a new kind in `platform/config`. Duplicate means the same host and the same port after normalization, regardless of `allow_private`; `host` and `host:443` are different entries, and `allow_private` applies to a request if one of the matching entries carries it. Messages name positions (`element 2`, `elements 1 and 3`), never values. The tests of the three keys and their environment names live in `internal/wire`. | `platform/config` has no project dependencies (M1-07) and knows no keys; the brief names `ParseRule` as the only parser. |
| M1-22/5 | 2026-10-11 | Grammar of `ParseRule` beyond the brief: labels from `a-z`, `0-9`, `-` and `_`, the last label is no number (`1.2.3`, `0x7f000001`), port without a leading zero, IPv6 in brackets also without a port, no zone, an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address becomes the IPv4 address. Hosts of request URLs go through the same grammar (uppercase becomes lowercase, a trailing dot is dropped); other forms (zone, characters outside ASCII) end with `url_not_allowed`. | `_` occurs in service names of Compose; resolvers following `inet_aton` read numeric names as an IPv4 address (the forms `a`, `a.b`, `a.b.c` and `a.b.c.d`, each part "in decimal, octal (with a leading 0), or hexadecimal", [inet_aton(3)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/inet_aton.3.html), retrieved 2026-10-12); one spelling per value, as for the kinds from M1-07. |
| M1-22/6 | 2026-10-11 | `egress.max_response_bytes` also limits the header lines of a response (`Transport.MaxResponseHeaderBytes`); a value of the consumer (`Options.MaxResponseBytes`) changes only the limit of the body. The body counts the bytes the caller reads, so decoded bytes for gzip negotiated by the transport (the transport decompresses such a response transparently, [Transport.DisableCompression](https://pkg.go.dev/net/http#Transport), retrieved 2026-10-12). | Otherwise the default of net/http (10 MiB, [transport.go](https://github.com/golang/go/blob/587fb2646e02a7304b845c352048312557f3bc94/src/net/http/transport.go#L333-L338), retrieved 2026-10-12) would apply to header lines without a named value; the default of 1 MiB equals the server's limit for request header lines. |
| M1-22/7 | 2026-10-11 | The decision of the check before the connection (a matching entry with `allow_private`) travels in the context of the request to the dialer. The dialer itself limits resolution and connection setup with the timeout, and the addresses of a name share the remaining time equally. The transport also sets the timeout as the deadline of every redirect step; `http.Client.Timeout` of `Do` and `HTTPClient` limits the whole chain. HTTP/2 stays off. | net/http detaches the dial from the cancellation of the request (Go 1.27, `context.WithoutCancel`, [transport.go](https://github.com/golang/go/blob/587fb2646e02a7304b845c352048312557f3bc94/src/net/http/transport.go#L1572), retrieved 2026-10-12); without a deadline of its own, a hanging resolution would continue (`TestTimeoutBoundsResolver`). Without the split, an address that never answers would use up the whole time (`TestDialSplitsTimeAcrossAddresses`, finding of the final review). The deadline in the transport also applies when a library puts the transport into its own client without a timeout, but then per step (`TestTransportDeadlineWithoutClientTimeout`, `TestTimeoutCoversAllHops`). The connection pool of net/http distinguishes scheme, host and port, from which the decision follows. |
| M1-22/8 | 2026-10-11 | `Client.Do` returns errors without path, query and user part (instead of `*url.Error`, an error with method, scheme and host that keeps `Unwrap` and `Timeout`) and, for a rejected redirect, only the error. | SEC-LOG-3: callers log errors; `*url.Error` carries the whole URL. |
| M1-22/9 | 2026-10-11 | Beyond the list of the brief, `bothy-go-egress-only` also reports references without a call (`get := http.Get`, `net.Dial` as a function value), `new(...)` of `http.Client`, `http.Transport`, `net.Dialer` and `tls.Dialer`, `tls.Dialer{...}`, `net.DialTCP`, `net.DialUDP` and `net.DialIP` (the last two since a review comment on pull request 15), `httputil.ReverseProxy` (also as a zero value, field or collection since a review comment on pull request 18, because its `Transport` "If nil, http.DefaultTransport is used": [net/http/httputil reverseproxy.go of Go 1.27.2](https://github.com/golang/go/blob/go1.27.2/src/net/http/httputil/reverseproxy.go), retrieved 2026-10-12) including `NewSingleHostReverseProxy` and `x.Transport = nil`, plus values of these four types (and of `httputil.ReverseProxy`) and their slices, arrays and maps (also with an array as the key) in `var`, literals, type definitions, aliases, named struct fields (also directly in an anonymous struct), anonymous structs and as an embedded field in gofmt form; embedded pointers stay allowed. Not covered are dot imports, renamed imports in the value forms (the explicit patterns of the list follow them), parameters and return values, deeper nesting and clients of third-party libraries that use `http.DefaultClient` internally; test code (`*_test.go`, `test/`, `tests/`, `testsuite/`, `services/*/perf/`) is exempt. | A zero-value client, a zero-value dialer and a reverse proxy bypass the check just like the cases of the brief (findings of the final review and the re-review, each with a `ruleid` or `ok` case in the fixture). A regular expression over the written type reads the value forms; Semgrep does not distinguish an embedded field from an embedded pointer, so a line rule checks the embedded value. A rule over the syntax does not see clients of third-party libraries; they get `Client.HTTPClient()`, and their ticket checks that. |

An environment value that starts with `[` is a JSON array by the list grammar from M1-07 (`TestEgressIPv6FirstNeedsJSONArray`). |
| M1-22/13 | 2026-10-11 | New file `.semgrepignore` with the built-in ignore list of Semgrep 1.180.0 ([default .semgrepignore](https://semgrep.dev/docs/ignoring-files-folders-code#understand-semgrep-defaults), retrieved 2026-10-12) without the test paths (`test/`, `tests/`, `testsuite/`, `*_test.go`). The rules meant only for product code (`bothy-go-no-model-credentials`, `bothy-go-sql-string-building`, `bothy-go-route-registration` and `bothy-go-egress-only`) exclude test paths in their own `paths`; their effect stays as before. | Without the file, `semgrep scan` skipped every test file, so `bothy-go-tls-verify` checked no test, contrary to the brief, which says "No path exclusion, also not for tests" (finding of the final review; proven with a throwaway copy: a test file with `InsecureSkipVerify: true` had no finding, with the file it is reported). |
| M1-22/14 | 2026-10-11 | A response `101 Switching Protocols` is an error for `Do`, `HTTPClient` and `NewLoopback`; a redirect (301, 302, 303, 307, 308) whose `Location` cannot be read ends with an error with a fixed text in both redirect modes. | After `101`, net/http hands over the raw connection as the body, and no deadline limits reading any more; an allowed target could hold goroutine and connection for any length of time (finding of the final review, `TestProtocolSwitchRefused`). net/http also turns an unreadable `Location` into an error with `http.ErrUseLastResponse` and quotes the header including path and query (`TestRedirectWithInvalidLocation`); with `RedirectNever`, the caller therefore gets an error instead of the 3xx response in this case. |

Sources: the IPv6 address of the instance metadata service is `[fd00:ec2::254]` ([Amazon EC2, Access instance metadata](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/instancedata-data-retrieval.html), "IPv6 support", retrieved 2026-10-12); the metadata server of the other provider answers at host `.200` of `100.100.100.0/24` ([Alibaba Cloud ECS, View instance metadata](https://www.alibabacloud.com/help/en/ecs/user-guide/view-instance-metadata), retrieved 2026-10-12). | R-SE-10 says that an operator entry opens an internal target, not the metadata of the clouds; before, both addresses lay only in ranges that `allow_private` opens, so a rule whose name resolves there reached the metadata service. ### M1-74 CodeQL for Go

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-74/1 | 2026-10-11 | Beyond the rules of the brief, `tools/ci/tests/test_codeql_workflow.py` checks further acceptance criteria of the workflow: setup-go stands before `init` and sets `cache: false` (also written as `'false'`, because setup-go reads the value as a boolean input: `core.getBooleanInput('cache')` in [setup-go src/main.ts](https://github.com/actions/setup-go/blob/main/src/main.ts), retrieved 2026-10-12), the build step sets `CGO_ENABLED: "0"`, `init` passes `languages: ${{ matrix.language }}` and `queries: security-extended`, and `analyze` follows `init` and uploads under `/language:${{ matrix.language }}`. The job and every step set only options from closed lists (`JOB_OPTIONS`, `STEP_OPTIONS`); every further step, also a second setup-go step, and a matrix axis next to `include` are findings. | Conditions are enforced, not assumed: otherwise only a review would hold what the brief justifies (no unverified cache, the shipped build configuration). Review and red team found, one after the other, options with which a matrix entry ends green without an analysis (a condition, `continue-on-error`, `upload: never`, a CodeQL configuration with path filters); instead of forbidding each one, the test knows every allowed option, and a new one is a deliberate change of the test. Next to an axis, GitHub merges the `include` entries with the combinations of the axis: an axis value without an entry runs without `build-mode`, and with a foreign axis a later entry overwrites values of an earlier one (an entry is added to every combination it does not overwrite, otherwise "it is added as an additional matrix combination", and a later entry "overwrites the `color: green` that was added by the previous `include` entry": [Running variations of jobs in a workflow, "Expanding or adding matrix configurations"](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/write-workflows/choose-what-workflows-do/run-job-variations#expanding-or-adding-matrix-configurations), retrieved 2026-10-12). Top-level keys, further jobs and the trigger `pull_request` are closed in the same way since M1-74/6. Not covered are the values of allowed keys without a rule of their own (such as `concurrency`, the value of `timeout-minutes`, the single permissions at the top and in the job, the triggers next to `pull_request`, keys under `strategy` next to `matrix`; `check_runners` checks its own rules for these, which its docstring lists, such as the runner switch, trigger rules, that top-level permissions are set and not `write-all`, that a timeout is set, and the pins), a job condition of the form `(<guard>) && ...`, which `check_runners.has_public_guard` accepts on purpose (the brief prescribes `has_public_guard`), and settings outside the file (code scanning of the repository on GitHub). The trial run in `docs/quality/public-switch.md` shows the settings outside the file. |
| M1-74/2 | 2026-10-11 | `test_public_switch_runbook_names_every_codeql_job` requires that step 18 "Trial run" in `docs/quality/public-switch.md` names the job `analyze (<language>)` of every matrix entry. | The runbook names the jobs that must be green after the switch to public; without a test, it would become incomplete unnoticed with the next matrix entry (`javascript-typescript`). |
| M1-74/3 | 2026-10-11 | The path patterns of `LANGUAGE_SOURCES` are evaluated with `fnmatch.fnmatchcase`: `*` also matches across `/`, and case matters on every operating system. | Test and Makefile find the same manifests (M1-74/5): the git pathspecs of the Makefile discovery also let `*` match across `/` and are case-sensitive ("in particular, `*` and `?` can match directory separators", [gitglossary, pathspec](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitglossary#Documentation/gitglossary.txt-aiddefpathspecapathspec), retrieved 2026-10-12; case-insensitive matching needs the magic word `icase`); `fnmatch.fnmatch` would not be on Windows ("Both parameters are case-normalized using `os.path.normcase()`", while `fnmatchcase` "is case-sensitive and does not apply `os.path.normcase()`"; "the filename separator (`'/'` on Unix) is not special to this module", [fnmatch](https://docs.python.org/3/library/fnmatch.html), retrieved 2026-10-12). |
| M1-74/4 | 2026-10-11 | The build step sets `CGO_ENABLED` as the YAML string `"0"`; an unquoted `0` and every other spelling of the number 0 (`0b0`, `0_0`, `00`) are findings. | `check_runners.load_yaml` resolves numbers by YAML 1.1 and reads all these spellings as the number 0. GitHub reads by YAML 1.2 [assumed, not checked offline] and passes `0b0` and `0_0` on as text (the integer forms of the core schema are decimal, `0o` and `0x` only: [YAML 1.2.2, 10.3.2 Tag Resolution](https://yaml.org/spec/1.2.2/#1032-tag-resolution), retrieved 2026-10-12), and Go takes only exactly `0` as off (`CGO_ENABLED=0b0 go env CGO_ENABLED` gives `1`); the build on the runner would run with cgo. After `load_yaml`, an unquoted `0` (also off for GitHub) can no longer be told from `0b0`; only the string is unambiguous, and the brief writes `"0"`. The string is compared as it stands: Go also accepts `" 0"` as on. `load_yaml` already reads booleans by YAML 1.2 (`no` stays text), so the rule for `cache: false` stays unchanged. |
| M1-74/5 | 2026-10-11 | The component patterns of `LANGUAGE_SOURCES` (`COMPONENT_MANIFESTS`) are the git pathspecs of the component discovery of the root Makefile, verbatim: `services/*go.mod`, `sdk/python/pyproject.toml`, `plugins/*pyproject.toml`, `sdk/typescript/package.json`, `plugins/*package.json`. `test_component_manifests_are_the_makefile_discovery` keeps them equal to `GO_MODS`, `PY_PROJECTS` and `TS_PROJECTS` and requires an assignment for every further discovery variable of the Makefile (`DART_PKGS`: none, CodeQL does not support Dart: the language table of [Supported languages and frameworks](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-overview/supported-languages-and-frameworks/), retrieved 2026-10-12, lists no Dart). A discovery is an assignment in a line without a leading tab (any operator, also after `export` or `override`, also across line continuations) whose value calls `$(dir ...)` or `${dir ...}` and `git ls-files`; its pathspecs are the arguments of every `git ls-files` in it without options and redirections, and `+=` appends. Not recognized are a component list without `$(dir ...)` or without `git ls-files` and one that is built through `define`, `$(eval ...)` or `$(call ...)`. The modules for the Go rules come from every pattern with the language `go`. The narrower patterns of the table in the brief (`services/*/go.mod`, `plugins/*/pyproject.toml`, `plugins/*/package.json`) no longer apply. That `*` in these pathspecs crosses `/` is the pathspec rule cited in M1-74/3. | The table of the brief names mirroring the Makefile discovery as its purpose, and each of its rows still maps the same way. With the narrower patterns, a tracked `services/go.mod` would be a Go module for the Makefile, on which all server gates run, but invisible to CodeQL; that is the silent gap the goal of the ticket rules out. Narrowing the statement to the planned layout would have left the gap open. |
| M1-74/6 | 2026-10-11 | `codeql.yml` sets at the top level only `name`, `on`, `permissions`, `concurrency` and `jobs` (`WORKFLOW_OPTIONS`); `jobs` contains only `analyze`; `on` contains `pull_request`, whose only allowed filter is `branches`; it then names `main` (as a list or a single value) and no pattern with `!` (`PULL_REQUEST_OPTIONS`). | The same error class as in M1-74/1, found for the third time: a top-level `env` (`GOFLAGS`, `GOTOOLCHAIN`, `CODEQL_ACTION_DEPENDENCY_CACHING`, `CODEQL_EXTRACTOR_GO_OPTION_EXTRACT_TESTS`) acts on every step and a top-level `defaults` (`run.shell`) on the build step, without changing a step; a further job would lie outside every rule and could upload under the categories of `analyze`; without `pull_request` or with a filter such as `paths` or `types`, pull requests would stay unanalysed without a red check, as with a later pattern such as `!main`, which GitHub applies after `main` ("A matching negative pattern (prefixed with `!`) after a positive match will exclude the Git ref", [workflow syntax, on.pull_request.branches](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#onpull_requestpull_request_targetbranchesbranches-ignore), retrieved 2026-10-12). The closed lists thus reach up to the top level of the file. |

Names are code spans as in Python-Markdown (backtick runs of equal length, also `<code>`); a known name (module, register entry, action, event type or op of the catalogue) outside a code span and raw HTML are findings. The check does not read what the site hides: code blocks and HTML comments (a `<!--` in a code span is text, a line that starts with three backticks and more backticks is no code block). The check recognizes a table as Python-Markdown does: a paragraph whose second line has as many cells and consists only of `\|`, `:`, `-` and spaces; every further line of the paragraph is a row of the table, and a pipe character in a code span splits no cell. It reads ATX headings as Python-Markdown does (`#` in the first column, also without a space after it); it does not read Setext headings. A level-4 heading under "Tables" in 03 is exactly one table name in backticks; a level-3 heading that starts with a code span and every heading of level 5 or 6 are findings. A title appears exactly once in its file. If a file, a section, a table or a column is missing, or if the register or the catalogue is invalid, the check ends with exit code 2 and one line. | Prose such as "all" must not silently become the empty set, and a name must not silently drop out of the comparison or get into it because the check reads it differently from the site. ### Leak gate for pull request text

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| LG/1 | 2026-10-12 | The report of the independent review stays local in `.review/<sha>.md`; the pull request (section "Review" of the template) names the reviewed commit, the verdict and the fixes in fresh words. Pull request title and body are written fresh, without raw reviewer, bot or agent output, local paths, user or machine names or temporary folder names. Replaces the "Review report" part of D-W-50. | The body becomes the squash commit message on `main` (push discipline, repository setting), so raw review output pasted into a body ends up in the public history of `main`, and a commit on `main` is not rewritten without the owner. |
| LG/2 | 2026-10-12 | `denylist_scan.py` checks `PR_TITLE` (all rules) and `PR_BODY` and `PR_BODY_FILE` (like a commit message: identity trailers only against the private list) when set; both deny-list jobs of `ci.yml` pass title and body from the event payload through the environment. A `Signed-off-by` line in the body passes only with a full identity `Name <email>` from `allow-authors.txt` (a bare allowed e-mail is not enough there); `Co-authored-by` and the text get the rules as in a commit. A `PR_BODY_FILE` that cannot be read is a configuration error (exit 2). | The squash commit does not exist when CI runs, so there is no author to compare the sign-off with; the allowed identity is the only one that may appear. Environment variables keep untrusted text out of the script (zizmor, template injection). Fail closed as for a missing list. |
| LG/3 | 2026-10-12 | `ci.yml` runs on `pull_request` types `opened`, `synchronize`, `reopened` and `edited`; an edit runs the whole workflow again, not only the deny-list jobs. The `ci-ok` job has no `needs`: it starts with the run and `tools/ci/ci_ok.py` waits for the other jobs through the API (`actions: read`, also granted by the `gates` call in `release.yml`) and applies the same rules as before. Residuals (event delivery delay, manual dispatch or re-run of an old run, text typed into the merge dialog) are listed in push-discipline.md; `denylist-main.yml` stays the check after the merge. | `ci-ok` is the only required check and is evaluated per head commit; a run that skipped `gates` on an edit would have to pass `ci-ok` without them. Red team (reproduced from run data): with `needs`, the check run of `ci-ok` appears only when `gates` ends, so for that whole time the older green `ci-ok` was the latest result and an edited body could be merged. The extra full run costs nothing: "GitHub Actions usage is free for self-hosted runners and for public repositories that use standard GitHub-hosted runners" ([GitHub Actions billing](https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions), retrieved 2026-10-12). |
| LG/4 | 2026-10-12 | CodeRabbit writes its high-level summary into the walkthrough comment (`high_level_summary_in_walkthrough: true`), not into the body. | With `edited` every bot edit of the body would restart CI, and the bot summary would become part of the commit message on `main`. https://docs.coderabbit.ai/reference/configuration, retrieved 2026-10-12 [verified]. |
| LG/5 | 2026-10-12 | An identity trailer counts only in the exact form `Signed-off-by: Name <local@domain>` or `Co-authored-by: Name <local@domain>` with a plain address (letters, digits and `._%+-` before the `@`, dot-separated labels after it). The private list checks the whole line; the other rules check the line without the local part of the address, so name and domain are still checked and only the contributor's own address is exempt. A trailer line with any other text is ordinary message text with all rules. Commits and tag messages are read with NUL as the record separator, so separator bytes inside a message stay message text. Applies to commit messages and pull request bodies alike. | Red team, two rounds (reproduced): first any line starting with `Signed-off-by:` skipped the generic rules, then any text inside `<...>` did (home path, private IP, tailnet name, ID), and an allowed bare e-mail let another name pass in a body. The class is fixed at one place: only the local part of a plain address is ever exempt. A message with the old separator bytes could hide text from every rule. |

One reader per chapter group collected the milestone of every key from PLAN and the M1 briefs, and a second independent checker recomputed every assignment (no difference). |
| M1-08/4 | 2026-10-11 | D7 counts a retired name in code and its `BOTHY_` form anywhere, each only as a whole name (`db.system.name`, `http.listen_addr_v2` and `BOTHY_DB__LOCK_TIMEOUT_X` are other names). Code is every line in a fenced code block (backticks or tildes, at any indentation) and every code span after CommonMark with GitHub tables (`code_parts`): a run of N backticks that is not escaped, up to the next run of exactly N, across line breaks of a paragraph, list item or quote, never across a blank line, a heading, a table row or table cell, or a new list item. These block rules apply to Markdown files; in other text files, where `#` starts a comment, only blank lines and fences end a paragraph. Indented code blocks and nested YAML do not count. Files with a NUL byte or invalid UTF-8 count as binary and are skipped. `docs/operations/configuration.md` is checked in the form that `make config-reference` writes. The allowlist of the brief is extended by `docs/briefs/M1-08-config-reference.md`; `docs/PLAN.md` stays exempt as a whole file. | "In backticks" (brief) means code in Markdown. Indented code blocks cannot be told apart reliably from indented list paragraphs without a full parser. The generated block follows the chapters, which D7 checks themselves; checked as on disk, it would have kept a name that was just retired. The brief of this ticket describes W1 like PLAN and names an old name in backticks; it is outside the scope. The brief exempts PLAN as a whole; an exception only for the W1 rows would depend on the table form of PLAN, which the planning session maintains. |

### M1-09 Secret provider and `bothy secrets`

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-09/1 | 2026-10-11 | The file driver removes exactly one `\n` at the end and a `\r` directly before it; otherwise the content stays unchanged. | `echo value > file` is the usual way to store a secret, and a line end is never part of a password; generated secrets are ASCII without a line end, and nothing changes for them. |
| M1-09/2 | 2026-10-11 | A file that is empty after this rule counts as not found (`not_found`), like an empty environment variable. Content over `maxFileSize` (`file.go`) is a read error (`read_failed`). | An empty password or key is never valid; the start thus aborts with a clear message instead of later in the database driver. The limit keeps a large file referenced by mistake out of memory. |
| M1-09/3 | 2026-10-11 | The driver `env` reads from the environment that `cmd/bothy` read once: `Options` has the additional field `Env` for it, `NewEnvDriver(environ)` looks names up there, and the first entry of a name applies as with `os.Getenv`. Apart from helper functions this is the only addition to the interface the brief sets. | The forbidigo rule (M1-07/10) and 02 "Configuration loading" item 5 allow reading the environment only in `cmd/bothy`. The environment of a process does not change from outside; every uncached `Get` therefore returns the same as `os.LookupEnv`. |
| M1-09/4 | 2026-10-11 | The placeholder `<secrets.dir>` is also replaced in set values (file, environment) of `*_ref` keys by the effective value of `secrets.dir`, other placeholders are not. This replaces the last sentence of M1-07/14. | The brief asks for the replacement in default and set values; Compose and operators can thus write `file:<secrets.dir>/db_owner_password`. More replacement would be a template language without a user. |
| M1-09/5 | 2026-10-11 | A reference `file:` needs an absolute path in clean form (`path.Clean` does not change it: no `.` or `..`, no double or trailing slash) without a NUL character, in the loader as in `secrets.ParseRef`. | `bothy config check` and the provider thus accept the same grammar; a reference that the check lets through does not fail only at start. |
| M1-09/6 | 2026-10-11 | Errors about an invalid reference never quote its text; they name only a known scheme (`file`, `env`, `azkv`, `awssm`) and the violated rule. Valid references appear in full. | As in M1-07: a secret may stand in the wrong key or the wrong column. A valid reference is configuration and also stands in `bothy config print`. |
| M1-09/7 | 2026-10-11 | The file driver first checks with `os.Stat` (follows symlinks) that a regular file is present, then opens it and checks kind, mode and owner again on the opened file (`File.Stat`, `os.SameFile`) before it reads. | The file cannot be swapped between check and read; a FIFO would block on open ("If O_NONBLOCK is clear, an open() for reading-only shall block the calling thread until a thread opens the file for writing", [POSIX open](https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/functions/open.html), retrieved 2026-10-12) and is rejected before. |
| M1-09/8 | 2026-10-11 | If a later read fails and a value is in the cache, the provider returns the old value, writes a log line, increments the counter once and sets the read time to now; the next attempt comes after `secrets.cache_ttl`. A clock that stands before the read time counts as expired. | One line per TTL and reference instead of one per call; the alert `BothySecretRefreshFailing` needs only an increase. After a clock jump back (NTP) a value would otherwise stay valid until the clock caught up. |
| M1-09/9 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets export` backs up exactly one directory (`--from`, in Compose `_store`). The files of `secrets.signing_key_dir` enter the bundle with M1-32, which generates the signing keys (brief M1-32, section 2). Format: POSIX tar (PAX) with relative names, mode bits, uid and gid 0 without names, mtime 0, sorted by name, with directories; encrypted with age to every X25519 recipient, optionally with ASCII armor. | Brief D8; before M1-32 there are no signing keys. A fixed format sorted by name, without owner names and times, reveals nothing about the host and is reproducible. |
| M1-09/10 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets export` rejects missing and invalid recipients (an invalid one named only by its number, never by its text) and binary output to a terminal without `--armor` with exit code 2, and an existing file for `--output` with exit code 1, which stays untouched. If the export fails, it deletes the output file it created. It never writes plaintext, not even a temporary file. | Brief D8; half a bundle would look like a valid backup. |
| M1-09/11 | 2026-10-11 | `totp_key` is a catalogue entry with the generator `pending` until M1-39 builds it (brief M1-39); the configuration reference names it among the generators of later milestones. | Every row of the table "Secrets" is a catalogue entry (brief D6); M1-39 sets the format of the key. |
| M1-09/12 | 2026-10-11 | Only regular files are allowed in `--source`; a symlink, a device or a FIFO aborts with exit code 2 and names the path. Files with the prefix `.bothy-tmp-` in target directories are leftovers of an interrupted run and are removed. | `secrets init` runs as root over a directory of the owner and follows no link there. An aborted run should reach the same state on the next run without manual steps. |
| M1-09/13 | 2026-10-11 | Before the first write, `bothy secrets init` checks: unknown files of the source, invalid or missing `--owner` (exit code 2), an `--owner` other than its own uid:gid without root (exit code 1) and conflicts of generated entries (exit code 1, all entries named). If one of these checks fails, it writes nothing. Every service directory of the catalogue is created, also without a file. | A half reconciled volume would be harder to repair than a rejected run. Compose aborts on a `subpath` that does not exist. |
| M1-09/14 | 2026-10-11 | W3: ruling L1 (D-L1) also covers `secrets`, `healthcheck` and `restore`; `bothy admin ...` in 08 is called `bothy setup bootstrap-owner` and `bothy setup issue-enrollment-code`. M1-37 changes 08. | Recommendation of the plan (PLAN W3); 02 "Commands of the binary" already lists the commands. |
| M1-09/15 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets.dir` is an absolute path in clean form, not `/`, without a NUL character and without `<`. The loader checks the rule for every supplied value and for the default (`config.checkSecretsDir`); a wrong value is a problem of `secrets.dir` alone, and references with `<secrets.dir>` then report no second one. `secrets.NewProvider` requires the same for `Options.Dir`, and `secrets.ParseRef` refuses `/` with the `secrets.dir` detail. The cross check `secrets_dir_clean_absolute` in `internal/wire`, built first, is dropped. | `bothy config check` should reject what the start rejects: an empty `secrets.dir` turned `file:<secrets.dir>/x` into the valid reference `file:/x`, `/` into the invalid `file://x`, and a `<` in the value would bring back, after the replacement, a placeholder that `Resolve` would replace a second time. As a cross check the rule ran only after the replacement and produced a misleading follow-up problem per reference (findings of the task reviews and of the closing review). |
| M1-09/16 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets init` holds an exclusive, non-blocking lock (`flock`) on the target directory for the whole run; a second run on the same target fails with exit code 1. Platforms without `flock` reject `init`. | Two concurrent runs (for example `docker compose run secrets-init ...` during `up`) deleted each other's temporary files and could replace a generated value with a second one, which the brief forbids (finding of a task review). |
| M1-09/17 | 2026-10-11 | The fingerprint of `secrets init` (twelve hex characters of an unsalted SHA-256) does not count as free of secrets: it confirms a guessed weak supplied value. The output, which the log of `secrets-init` keeps, does not belong in public places; the README of `stack` says so. | The brief sets format and length (16 5.3 item 5); generated values have 256 bits of randomness and are not affected. |
| M1-09/18 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets init` with an empty `--target` or `--source` and `bothy secrets export` with an empty `--from` or `--output` are usage errors (exit code 2). | As M1-07/21: an empty path from an empty variable would silently mean "no source" (a restore with `--adopt-source` would generate new secrets instead of taking over the bundle) or standard output instead of a file. For the same reason `--adopt-source` without `--source` is a usage error: without a source the flag has no effect (finding of the closing review). |
| M1-09/19 | 2026-10-11 | A second `--owner` for the same service is a usage error (exit code 2). | The command does not guess which of two owners is meant. |
| M1-09/20 | 2026-10-11 | Usage errors that only `stack` detects (every error with `stack.ErrUsage`: an unknown or non-regular file of the source, a path of `--source` or `--from` that is missing or not a directory, `--owner` for an unknown service, a missing `--owner` for `server` or `migrate`, a missing or invalid recipient, binary output to a terminal without `--armor`) give exit code 2 with exactly one line `bothy secrets <subcommand>: <error>` without the help text; errors of the command line itself (unknown flag, invalid flag value, missing `--target` or `--from`, empty path, unexpected argument, missing or unknown subcommand) also show the help. | The line names the file, service or recipient; the help would hide it and does not help, because the form of the call was right (deviation from the plan, which always showed the help). |
| M1-09/21 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets` without a subcommand is a usage error like `bothy` and `bothy config` without a command (exit code 2, help on standard error); `help`, `-h` and `--help` print the help with exit code 0. Apart from the paths of `--target`, `--source`, `--from` and `--output`, which messages name, no message of `bothy secrets` repeats text of the command line: an unexpected argument, an unknown flag or an invalid flag value appear only with their position (`argument 3 ...`), an unknown subcommand without its name, and `--owner` accepts only service names of lower-case letters, digits and `-`, so that an unknown service is never other text. | A call that does nothing must not count as success in a script with an empty variable. An age identity pasted without `--age-recipient` (`AGE-SECRET-KEY-1...`) must reach the terminal or a log as little as a rejected recipient, which `stack.Export` names only by its number (M1-09/10). |
| M1-09/22 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy config check` warns, with and without `--secrets`, on standard error about every key `*_ref` whose effective reference uses `env:`; the exit code stays unaffected. | Brief D2: `env:` is allowed but not recommended (16 5.2); the check without resolving is the usual call and should show the hint as well (finding of a task review). |
| M1-09/23 | 2026-10-11 | The last line of `secrets init` is the export command that can be called, with the `_store` of the run: `back up the secrets: bothy secrets export --from <target>/_store --age-recipient <age public key>`; a path that is not a simple shell word stands in single quotes. | M1-09/24 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets.cache_ttl` is at least `1s`; `Options.CacheTTL` may be `0` for tests. | With `0` every call would read the secret, and while a later read fails, every call would write a log line and increment the counter instead of once per TTL (finding of the closing review). |

### M1-12 PostgreSQL access, migrations and schema version

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-12/1 | 2026-10-11 | Role passwords reach PostgreSQL only as SCRAM-SHA-256 verifiers computed in Go: 4096 iterations, 16 random bytes of salt (`scramIterations`, `scramSaltBytes` in `scram.go`), sent with the simple protocol so pgx never caches the statement. | 4096 is the default of `scram_iterations` ([PostgreSQL, scram_iterations](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/runtime-config-connection.html#GUC-SCRAM-ITERATIONS), retrieved 2026-10-12) and the minimum iteration count of [RFC 7677](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7677) section 4 (retrieved 2026-10-12), so a login costs what it costs with a password PostgreSQL hashed itself; 16 bytes is the salt PostgreSQL generates (`SCRAM_DEFAULT_SALT_LEN` in [scram-common.h](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/master/src/include/common/scram-common.h), retrieved 2026-10-12). |
| M1-12/2 | 2026-10-11 | Role passwords must be non-empty printable ASCII (0x20 to 0x7e); the error names the role, never the value. | For those characters SASLprep (PostgreSQL, libpq) and OpaqueString (pgx) change nothing, so every client derives the key the verifier was computed from; `secrets init` generates base64url values. |
| M1-12/3 | 2026-10-11 | `pg.Config` renders every libpq setting in the connection string, also empty ones (`passfile`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`, `sslrootcert`, `channel_binding`, `require_auth` and the rest), drops fallbacks, Kerberos names and the parsed password afterwards, and sets pgx's exec mode, cache sizes and the pool lifetimes to their defaults, so a libpq service file (`PGSERVICE`) cannot change them. | pgx reads `PG*` variables, `~/.pgpass`, `~/.pg_service.conf` and `~/.postgresql/`; 02 "Configuration loading" item 5 allows only `cmd/bothy` to read the environment, and a stray `PGSSLMODE` or `root.crt` must not change a connection. |
| M1-12/4 | 2026-10-11 | A zero deadline in `pg.Config` switches it off as in PostgreSQL; the configuration keys have `1ms` as minimum and `24d` as maximum (`database.statement_timeout`, `lock_timeout`, `idle_in_tx_timeout`, `migrate.lock_timeout`). Deadlines are sent in whole milliseconds, rounded up. | `bothy migrate` needs `statement_timeout = 0`; the server never may (02 "Deadlines"). PostgreSQL stores the three as int32 milliseconds; rounding up keeps a positive deadline below 1ms from becoming 0. |
| M1-12/5 | 2026-10-11 | Every connection starts with `default_transaction_isolation = 'read committed'` and `TimeZone = 'UTC'`; `Begin` sets READ COMMITTED when the options leave the level empty. | 03 "Principles" item 11 and 16 7.2 fix the level, and goose writes its `tstamp` with `now()` in the session time zone. |
| M1-12/6 | 2026-10-11 | `Acquire`, `Begin` (acquire and `BEGIN` together) and `Ping` wait at most `database.acquire_timeout`. When that deadline passes while every connection is acquired, the cause is `db_pool_exhausted`; when the database does not answer or refuses the login, `dependency_unavailable`; both have code `unavailable`. An expired request context is `deadline_exceeded`, a canceled one comes back as the context error. | 02 "Error kinds" names `db_pool_exhausted` for "no connection was free"; a database that is down is a different operator signal. A client that went away is no server error. |
| M1-12/7 | 2026-10-11 | `WaitReady` and `pg.Connect` retry with exponential backoff from `backoffBase` to `backoffCap` (`pool.go`) and full jitter; each failed attempt logs `database not reachable yet, retrying` with `server.address`, `server.port` and `error.type` (SQLSTATE, secret error code, `timeout` or `connect`), never the error text. The final error names host and port and wraps the last real cause; an attempt cut short by the end of the wait does not replace it. | Agreed with M1-10 (log keys of the catalogue, no foreign error text in logs, signals.md "Logs"); the final error goes to the operator on stderr. |
| M1-12/8 | 2026-10-11 | The tracer of the pool wraps otelpgx: it replaces `PASSWORD '...'` literals in the statement text with `PASSWORD '[redacted]'`, and the error of a failed statement, batch, copy or prepare with a PostgreSQL error that keeps severity and SQLSTATE only. | otelpgx writes `err.Error()` into the span status and an exception event, and PostgreSQL messages quote parameter values (`invalid input syntax for type integer: "<value>"`), which the parameter option alone does not keep out; the role statements of the bootstrap carry the SCRAM verifier as a literal, enough for an offline guess. |
| M1-12/9 | 2026-10-11 | otelpgx v0.13.0 has no option for semantic conventions and emits only the current names: `db.system.name`, `db.namespace`, `db.operation.name`, `db.query.text`, `db.collection.name` (COPY spans only), plus `server.address`, `server.port`, `user.name`, `pgx.rows_affected`, `pgx.sql_state` and `pgx.prepare_stmt.name` ([otelpgx v0.13.0 tracer.go](https://github.com/exaring/otelpgx/blob/v0.13.0/tracer.go), retrieved 2026-10-12). | signals.md "Database" asks to check the installed version; the span allowlist of M1-10 drops `user.name` and `pgx.*` before export. |
| M1-12/10 | 2026-10-11 | The migrator uses goose's PostgreSQL session locker with a poll every `lockPollSeconds` seconds for up to `lockPollAttempts` attempts (`migrator.go`), a fresh locker per migrator, and the global goose registry switched off. | Two `migrate up` apply each migration once; goose keeps the retry state of its polls in the locker. |
| M1-12/11 | 2026-10-11 | The schema version is `max(version_id)` of the applied rows of `goose_db_version`, 0 without the table; `CheckSchema` and `Migrator.Status` read it with their own queries. | goose's `GetDBVersion` and `Status` take the migration lock and create the version table; a status or a start check must only read. |
| M1-12/12 | 2026-10-11 | Against a database newer than the binary, `migrate up` runs neither its steps nor goose; it prints `schema is newer than this binary; nothing to do` and exits 0. | upgrade.md "Rollback": the older binary must not touch a schema of the newer one, also not through River or the instance row. |
| M1-12/13 | 2026-10-11 | Output of `bothy migrate`: `applied <version> <name>` with the version as a number and the slug of the file name, then `database version <n>, applied <k>`; status lines `<version> <name> <applied\|pending> <applied_at\|->` with the time in RFC 3339 UTC; `bootstrap: roles and grants are in place` after `--bootstrap`; `migrations ok` after `validate`. A configuration that does not load or bind is exit code 2, every runtime failure 1. | Stable lines for scripts on stdout (02 3.3); 02 3.3 counts configuration errors as exit code 2. |
| M1-12/14 | 2026-10-11 | `Validate` parses each file with goose's own parser through a provider over a driver that refuses every statement, requires `-- +goose Up`, and refuses `-- +goose ENVSUB` in any case and after any number of `--`, as goose reads it. | goose v3.28.0 has no public parser (its SQL parser is the internal package `internal/sqlparser`, [goose v3.28.0](https://github.com/pressly/goose/tree/v3.28.0/internal/sqlparser), retrieved 2026-10-12); with ENVSUB a migration would read the process environment at migrate time. |
| M1-12/15 | 2026-10-11 | `BootstrapRoles` runs in one transaction under a transaction advisory lock and is retried once when a concurrent bootstrap created a role first (42710, 23505); `BootstrapDatabase` refuses a connection that is not on the named database. | Two `migrate up --bootstrap` must not fail each other; the schema statements act on the current database only. |
| M1-12/16 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy config check --secrets` prints `secret <key>: skipped (read only by bothy migrate up --bootstrap)` for the `migrate.*` references and does not resolve them. | The api and worker containers do not get the superuser and owner passwords; the runbook command would otherwise fail in every server container. |

A template whose migration fails is dropped again, so a retry reports the migration error, not "database already exists". Tests that change a role password do not run in parallel and restore it. | Templates make each database cheap; the deadlines leave room on a shared machine under the race detector; roles are cluster-wide. |
