### M1-10 Logging and telemetry foundation

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-10/1 | 2026-10-11 | D1: with `telemetry.enabled = false`, with `OTEL_LOGS_EXPORTER=none` or with `telemetry.logs.exporter = stdout` the server logger writes JSON to stdout even when `telemetry.logs.exporter = otlp`; there is no configuration in which logs go nowhere. | Brief D1: logs are never silently lost. An operator who turns telemetry off still needs the logs of the process (`TestDisabledTelemetryKeepsStdoutLogs`). |
| M1-10/2 | 2026-10-11 | D3: traces and logs each have one in-flight budget (`OTEL_BSP_MAX_QUEUE_SIZE`, `OTEL_BLRP_MAX_QUEUE_SIZE`, default 2048) across the front queue, the SDK batch processor and the running export. An item takes a slot on entering the front queue and returns it after its export; an item without a slot is counted in `bothy.telemetry.dropped{bothy.signal}`, and every item of a failed export (spans, log records, metric data points) is counted too. The SDK queues are as large as the budget, so neither the blocking span batch processor nor the log batch processor (no blocking option, it drops its oldest record on overflow) ever overflows. Not counted: items still queued when `Shutdown` reaches its deadline; a failed last metric export at `Shutdown` is counted but never exported. | A front queue alone is not enough: the SDK batch processors collect up to one export batch before they export, so with a collector that never answers they would hold that many items more than the front queue before anything was counted, and the log batch processor would then drop silently. One budget per signal bounds memory and makes every loss before the deadline visible. |
| M1-10/3 | 2026-10-11 | `Deps` has a fifth field `Env` (the environment as `cmd/bothy` read it). `Setup` reads from it the protocol variables (`grpc` default, `http/protobuf`; `http/json` has no Go exporter and is a start error), `OTEL_{TRACES,METRICS,LOGS}_EXPORTER` (`otlp` or `none`, anything else a start error) and the two queue sizes (a whole number of at least 1). Endpoint, headers, timeouts, TLS and `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` are still read by the OTLP exporters and package `resource` from the process environment. | Like M1-09/3: forbidigo allows reading the environment only in `cmd/bothy`. The vendor packages read their variables themselves; in the server both views are the same environment. |
| M1-10/4 | 2026-10-11 | `Middleware` is a package function as the brief fixes it; it reads per request the state of the last `Setup` (trust of the remote parent, server logger) from an atomic pointer. Before any `Setup` a remote parent is not trusted and the access log goes through `slog.Default()` behind the allowlist filter. `serve` (M1-11) calls `Setup` before it builds the chain. | The brief gives `Middleware(next)` no telemetry argument; a snapshot keeps the request path free of locks. |
| M1-10/5 | 2026-10-11 | The log allowlist filter resolves `LogValuer` values and flattens groups (`slog.Group`, `WithGroup`) into dotted keys; a flattened key must be `attr.Allowed`, and only scalar values (string, number, bool, duration, time, `[]string`) pass, so an error or struct value is dropped even under an allowed key. The span exporter keeps only scalar attribute values as well. | One key form in JSON and OTLP that matches the catalog and the collector allowlist; a group must not smuggle in a key the catalog does not list, and an encoder would write the text of an error, which signals.md "Logs" keeps out. |
| M1-10/6 | 2026-10-11 | Span export filter: `tracetest.SpanStubFromReadOnlySpan(...).Snapshot()` rebuilds every span with only allowed keys on the span, its events and links, clears the status description and, on server spans, removes `server.address` and `server.port`. | The pinned SDK v1.47.0 has no supported span attribute filter (brief D5). otelhttp fills `server.address` and `server.port` of a server span from the Host header of the client, an open and client-controlled set; the status description is free text. |
| M1-10/7 | 2026-10-11 | A method outside the nine standard HTTP methods is `_OTHER` in the access log and on the duration metric and `HTTP` in span names (as the HTTP semantic conventions do). The view of `http.server.request.duration` keeps exactly `http.request.method`, `http.route`, `http.response.status_code` and `error.type` with the boundaries of signals.md 4.7, drops the otelhttp body size histograms and turns exemplars off. `error.type` of a 5xx response is its status code. | Metric attributes only from closed sets (17 rule 9, signals.md "Cardinality"): otelhttp would add `server.address` from the Host header, and exemplars would carry trace ids and the filtered attributes. The body size histograms are not in the catalog. |
| M1-10/8 | 2026-10-11 | The OpenTelemetry error handler writes the constant message `telemetry export error` with `error.type` set to the Go type of the error, at most once per `exportErrorInterval` (`setup.go`) and only to stdout. `ServerErrorLog` maps every line of net/http to `http server error` with `error.type` `tls_handshake`, `accept`, `panic` or `other`. | Exporter errors quote endpoints and foreign messages; an OTLP record about a failed export could fail the same way, and a collector that is down would otherwise write a line per export. net/http lines carry client addresses (SEC-LOG-5). |
| M1-10/9 | 2026-10-11 | `NewPseudonymizer(ctx, keys, ref, rotation, clock)` and `(*Pseudonymizer).PseudonymWithPeriod` are exported besides the API of the brief; `KeySource` is the one-method interface `*secrets.Provider` satisfies. A principal id must be a UUID in canonical text form (any letter case); the period index rounds toward negative infinity. | `bothy telemetry pseudonym` needs the pseudonym and its period start without `Setup`, which would start exporters. privacy.md fixes the input as the lowercase UUID; another form would give a pseudonym nobody can look up. |
| M1-10/10 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy telemetry pseudonym <principal_id> [--at TIME] [--config FILE]`: output `pseudonym <hex>` and `period_start <RFC 3339 UTC>` on stdout; exit 0, 1 when the key cannot be read or is shorter than `otel.MinKeyBytes`, 2 for a usage error, a principal id that is not a UUID or a configuration that does not load. Flags may follow the id. | 02 "Commands of the binary": 2 is a call or configuration error. Unlike `config check`, whose result is the check itself, a configuration that does not load is a wrong call here. |
| M1-10/11 | 2026-10-11 | Commands log through `otel.NewCommandLogger` on stderr: `config check --secrets` and `telemetry pseudonym` at `telemetry.logs.level`, `info` before the configuration is loaded. `secrets init` and `export` do not log. | Brief scope `cmd/bothy`: the same allowlist for every output. |
| M1-10/12 | 2026-10-11 | Registered configuration keys (M1-07 had not): `telemetry.enabled`, `telemetry.traces.sampler_ratio` (0 to 1), `telemetry.traces.trust_remote_parent`, `telemetry.logs.exporter`, `telemetry.logs.level`, `telemetry.pseudonym_key_ref` (chapter 17), `privacy.telemetry_pseudonym_rotation` (chapter 20, at least `1s`) and `instance.environment` (chapter 16), defaults from the chapter tables. `telemetry.dashboard_url` comes with the admin console that links it. | The period index divides by whole seconds. `TestConfigKeysMatchChapterTables` holds the defaults. |
| M1-10/13 | 2026-10-11 | `sloglint` (`static-msg`, `attr-only`, `no-raw-keys`, `context: scope`, verified with `golangci-lint config verify`) checks product code; tests are exempt. `platform/secrets` keeps its own constant for `error.type`, and `kernel/errs` constants for the keys of its `LogValue` group. | Tests log arbitrary keys on purpose to prove the handlers drop them. `platform/otel` depends on `platform/secrets`, and platform cycles are not allowed (`architecture/modules.yaml`), so `secrets` cannot import `otel/attr`. |
| M1-10/14 | 2026-10-11 | `attr.Allowed` is false for the resource key `bothy.server.role` and the metric-only key `bothy.signal`; both are constants of `attr` with their scope. `genai.ConventionRef` names the convention repository and its status as read on 2026-10-10 without a commit. | signals.md lists `bothy.server.role` as a resource attribute and `bothy.signal` only on `bothy.telemetry.dropped`. signals.md records no commit of the conventions, and the brief forbids inventing one. |
| M1-10/15 | 2026-10-11 | The span export filter also clears the trace state of every span, its parent and its links; propagation to outgoing calls keeps it. | The trace state comes from the `tracestate` header of the client, free text outside the header allowlist; a probe of the closing review showed it on exported links (default) and spans (with `trust_remote_parent`). `TestTraceStateNeverExported`. |
| M1-10/16 | 2026-10-11 | After the handler, the access log step writes the route it used back to `r.Pattern` of the request `otelhttp` passed on (`""` for `unmatched`). | otelhttp takes `http.route` of the duration metric from `r.Pattern`, and that value wins over the labeler; below an outer `ServeMux` with a prefix pattern and `http.StripPrefix` the metric showed the prefix while span and log showed the route of `RecordRoute` (`TestRecordRouteWinsOverOuterPattern`). |
| M1-10/17 | 2026-10-11 | A handler panic passes `Middleware` unchanged and is re-raised in the deferred call that caught it; before that the access log and the span record status 500 (or the status already sent) with `error.type`. The duration metric does not count a panicking request. | The recovery of 02 9.1 step 1 sits outside step 2 and answers the request; its stack must still show the panicking frame (`TestPanicKeepsAccessLog` checks both). otelhttp records the metric only when the handler returns; counting it anyway would mean answering inside step 2, which is the job of step 1. |
| M1-10/18 | 2026-10-12 | Ported onto the English `main`: where M1-10 and M1-12 both extend a list (config key groups in `internal/wire/config.go`, the expected keys of `TestConfigSchemaBuilds`, the golden output of `bothy config print`, the help text of `bothy`, platform entries of the module register), the result holds both; the rules `instance.`, `telemetry.` and `privacy.telemetry_pseudonym_rotation` leave `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml`, because their keys enter the schema (D6); `internal/wire/routes.go` (M1-17) names the key of the "route check failed" record through the constant `logKeyError` (value `error`), so sloglint `no-raw-keys` passes without changing the record (M1-17/7); comments and tests name the English chapters and headings (the collector test reads signals.md section "3. Resource attributes"); `go mod tidy` and the generated files are run again on this stack. | All repository content is English (D-W-94); the German integration commit recorded the same list unions and the sloglint constant in two rulings of its own, which this note replaces. The help column of `bothy` is aligned to `telemetry`, and the `config check --secrets` test with the server schema expects the skipped `migrate.*` references of M1-12 and the resolved `telemetry.pseudonym_key_ref`, in key order. `go mod tidy` raises the indirect `go-openapi/jsonpointer` to v1.0.0 (dropping `swag/jsonname`), `golang.org/x/mod` to v0.41.0 and `golang.org/x/sync` to v0.23.0, and the direct `otelhttp` v0.72.0 replaces the indirect v0.71.0; the OTel exporters bring `golang.org/x/crypto` v0.57.0, `golang.org/x/text` v0.42.0 and `golang.org/x/net` v0.60.0; `go generate ./...` leaves the REST code unchanged. The constant keeps the key `error`, which `TestServe_AbortsOnUnknownAction` reads; switching to `error.type` would drop the operation and action names that M1-17/7 promises. M1-11, which wires the route step to the server logger of M1-10, decides how the joined error reaches the operator, because the allowlist handler drops the key `error` by design (signals.md "Logs"). |

### M1-11 Lifecycle, listeners, health and shutdown

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-11/1 | 2026-10-12 | D4: the runner listens for `SIGTERM` and `SIGINT` with a buffer of two. The first signal starts the orderly shutdown, also during the start: the start context ends, the components started so far are stopped through the plan, and the exit code is `0`. A second signal at any time ends with `3` without further steps. The end of the context of `Run` counts like a first signal. | Brief D4 and 02 "Shutdown". A signal during a long database wait is an operator stopping the process, not a failed start; `1` would make an orchestrator report a crash. |
| M1-11/2 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.Serve(ctx, *config.Values, Role, Deps)`: the typed configuration of M1-07 is `*config.Values`; a type `config.Config` does not exist. Each package binds its own `Config` struct (`httpserver.Config`, `wire.ServeConfig`, `otel.Config`, `pg.Config`). `Deps` carries the environment, stdout, clock, signals, version, the migration set (tests) and the module handlers. | The brief names `*config.Config` with the note "use the typed configuration type M1-07 created". |
| M1-11/3 | 2026-10-12 | The secret provider is built in `Serve` before any component, because `otel.Setup` resolves `telemetry.pseudonym_key_ref` through it. The start component `secrets` then resolves `database.password_ref` once, before the internal listener opens, so a missing or unreadable password file ends the start at once with exit `1` instead of after `startup.dependency_wait`. | 02 "Start" puts telemetry first and secrets second, and 02 "Failure of a dependency" makes the secret provider fail fast. The provider itself resolves lazily. |
| M1-11/4 | 2026-10-12 | The line `server ready` carries `bothy.server.role` as the brief asks, but the attribute catalog of `platform/otel` keeps that key on the resource only, so the stdout JSON drops it and OTLP carries it on the resource of every record. The listener lines carry `server.address` and `server.port`, which the catalog allows on logs. | signals.md "Resource attributes" and `attr.Allowed` are the contract of M1-10; widening the catalog is outside this ticket. The role is still on every exported record. |
| M1-11/5 | 2026-10-12 | Stop step budgets: `0` means the step is bounded only by the rest of `shutdown.total_timeout` (readiness, pool, internal listener). A step that outlives its budget is left running and the plan moves on with the log line `shutdown step timed out`; a step error is logged as `shutdown step failed` and does not change the exit code; a panicking step counts as failed. Exit `1` comes only from a total that is spent. Steps of the same phase run in reverse registration order. The budget sum is checked when `Run` begins, over the steps registered by then; `wire.Serve` registers all its steps before `Run`. | 02 "Shutdown": exit `1` is reserved for a shutdown over the total. A hanging step must not hold the whole plan; the orchestrator's longer grace period remains the backstop. |
| M1-11/6 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.Serve` calls `MountRoutes` as start step 7 now, because M1-17 is on the base; with no enabled operation it mounts nothing. The public listener serves `httpserver.Public(mux)`: a request no route matches gets `404` with an empty body; a method mismatch also gets `404` until M1-18 brings the problem format. The fallback asks `ServeMux.Handler` for the pattern instead of registering a catch-all route, so the Semgrep rule `bothy-go-route-registration` needs no exception on the public side. The internal mux registers `/healthz` and `/readyz` with a `nosemgrep` comment that names R-AZ-22. | M1-17/7 promised that M1-11 calls the step before the public listener opens. A catch-all pattern on the public mux would bypass the route check the rule protects. |
| M1-11/7 | 2026-10-12 | Bounds of the new keys: `http.read_header_timeout`, `http.idle_timeout`, `shutdown.http_grace` and `shutdown.telemetry_flush_timeout` at least `1ms`; `shutdown.drain_delay` at least `0s`; `shutdown.total_timeout` at least `1s`; every `shutdown.*` key at most `1h`; `http.max_header_bytes` from `4096` to `16777216`. | A zero would switch the limit off in net/http or leave a step without its own budget. One hour bounds the sum, so it cannot overflow. Below 4 KiB ordinary requests with bearer token and DPoP proof no longer fit; 16 MiB bounds the memory one connection can bind. |
| M1-11/8 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy serve` writes its JSON log lines to stdout (the stdout exporter of 17, `otel.Deps.Stdout`) and the cause of a failed start, for example the hint to run `bothy migrate up`, as one line `bothy serve: <error>` on stderr. The log handlers keep no error text. | 02 "Commands of the binary" puts logs of commands on stderr so scripts can read stdout; `serve` prints nothing for scripts, and 17 defines stdout as the log output of the server. The stderr line is the carrier of the error text that INT-W2/7 left to M1-11. |
| M1-11/9 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy healthcheck` checks the URL itself before any request (scheme `http`, no user info, host `localhost` or a loopback address without zone) and quotes nothing of it in errors; the loopback client of `platform/egress` checks again at connection time. Exit `1` writes one line: `status <code>`, `timeout`, `connection failed` or `refused: <reason>`. Redirects are not followed (a `3xx` is exit `1`). The default `--timeout` of `5s` equals the Compose healthcheck timeout; a test reads both. | Brief D5. Error texts of net/http carry addresses; a class is enough for a probe. |
| M1-11/10 | 2026-10-12 | A listener whose `Serve` ends with an error other than the orderly close reports it through `Runner.Fail`: orderly shutdown, exit `1`. `/healthz` has no liveness check yet; the log tail, hub and River client report through `Fail` in their tickets (02 "Panics"). | 02 "Health": `/healthz` needs no own check because a failed core part ends the process. |
| M1-11/11 | 2026-10-12 | Ticket branch `feat/M1-11-lifecycle-health-shutdown` (name of the launcher instead of the brief) on the head of `int/m1-wave2`; this session does not merge it, the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the boundaries of the brief for branch name and merge. |
| M1-11/12 | 2026-10-12 | Red team (verdict JA, four Minors): the total of the shutdown now counts from the shutdown request instead of the first stop step; the drain step returns at once when the public listener never opened; the telemetry step switches the logger back to stdout before the flush, so the last lines survive a flush that outlives its budget. The English addition in the German command table of 02 stays. | The first three are reproduced by `TestTotalCountsFromTheShutdownRequest` and `TestServeSignalDuringStartSkipsTheDrain` or follow from the code; the table row of `telemetry pseudonym` set the precedent of English text in that table while the spec is being translated. |
| M1-11/13 | 2026-10-12 | Scoped re-review of the fixes (verdict JA, two Minors and a note): a spent shutdown total is now an error of `Runner.Err` naming the total and the phase, so `bothy serve` prints a cause on stderr for that exit `1` too, and `TestTotalCountsFromTheShutdownRequest` checks the log line, the skipped step and that error. The order "switch the logger, then flush" in the telemetry stop step gets no test of its own. | A test would need an OTLP endpoint that accepts and never answers; the order is two lines with a comment, and the effect (lines on stdout after a hanging flush) does not change an exit code. |
| M1-11/14 | 2026-10-12 | Port onto `main`: `TestServeReadyAfterStart` waits for the line `server ready` (`child.waitLine`) instead of reading stdout once after `/readyz` passed, and finds the public listener through `child.port`. This is the test fix M1-25 made on its branch (red team round 1), taken into the ticket whose test it is. | The child's stdout reaches the buffer through a pipe and readiness passes before the line is written, so a line can still be in flight when `/readyz` answers `200`; under load the test failed with "no ready line" while it passed alone. |
| M1-11/15 | 2026-10-13 | `lifecycle.StopStep.Wait` marks a step whose purpose is to wait for its budget; when the budget is spent the runner waits up to `waitGrace`, bounded by what the total leaves after the budget, for it to return and logs no timeout. The drain step is such a step and returns when its context ends; with `shutdown.drain_delay` 0 it returns at once. | Review comment on pull request 29: the drain step and its context both ended after the drain delay, so every graceful shutdown with a delay could log `shutdown step timed out`. `TestWaitStepReachingItsBudgetIsNoTimeout` failed before the change. |

### M1-13 Unit of work with phase hooks and in-transaction events

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-13/1 | 2026-10-12 | The module of a hook, handler or event type is the path segment after the last `/internal/` of its Go package (`_test` suffix dropped); `wire` and `platform` yield no module, and `kernel` may publish events but owns no hook or handler. The unit-of-work tests register through the stand-in module `internal/platform/pg/internal/fakemodule`, which lies inside the `platform-pg` component of the architecture gate; the tests of `kernel/txn` store registrations through the unexported half of `SetHook` and `Subscribe` and test the derivation on runtime symbol names. | Product packages have exactly one `/internal/`, so the rule equals "segment after /internal/" of the brief; the last occurrence lets a nested fixture act as a module without a test-only switch of the derivation. A fixture below `internal/kernel/txn` would belong to no component, and `make arch` rejects such files. |
| M1-13/2 | 2026-10-12 | A subscription is allowed only when the publisher is `kernel` or is listed directly in `depends_on` of the subscriber; a module subscribing to its own events and a subscriber or publisher missing from the register fail the check. | The subscriber imports the publisher's port for the event type, and go-arch-lint allows exactly the direct `depends_on` edges; an own event needs no bus. |
| M1-13/3 | 2026-10-12 | `CheckTxnDirections` takes `wire.ModuleDeps` (module to `depends_on`); the golden test reads `architecture/modules.yaml` with `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3`. | `wire` has no vendor groups in the register, and `tools/archgen` is a `main` package; test files are outside the architecture gate, and no second YAML library is added. |
| M1-13/4 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.Modules` is the (still empty) container of module objects, and `wire.NewTxnRegistry` the one function every role calls; `bothy serve` (M1-11) wires it. | No role builder exists yet; one function for all roles keeps the golden file the list of every role. |
| M1-13/5 | 2026-10-12 | A retry starts at once, without backoff; the deadlock victim then waits for the winner's locks under `database.lock_timeout`. | 02 names no pause and no key for one; the lock wait is already bounded. |
| M1-13/6 | 2026-10-12 | The rollback uses the request context without its cancellation, bounded by `database.statement_timeout`; its own error is dropped. | A canceled request must still end its transaction cleanly; the attempt already fails with the better error, and pgx closes a connection whose rollback failed ("A rollback failure leaves the connection in an undefined state", `dbTx.Rollback` in [pgx v5.11.0 tx.go](https://github.com/jackc/pgx/blob/v5.11.0/tx.go), retrieved 2026-10-13). |
| M1-13/7 | 2026-10-12 | The error after the last retry has code `unavailable` without an internal cause and without `Retry-After`; the HTTP mapping (M1-18) adds `http.unavailable_retry_after`. | 14.1 lists no cause for an exhausted retry, and this ticket returns only `kernel/errs` kinds. |
| M1-13/8 | 2026-10-12 | Exported besides the API of the brief: `txn.Options`, `ApplyOptions`, `Options.Covers`, `NewUnit`, `Key.Name`, `Registry.Hook`, `KindHook`, `KindSubscribe`, `ModuleOfSymbol`, `ModuleOfPackage`; `pg.DBTX` and `Exec`, `Query`, `QueryRow` on `pg.Pool`; `otel.SubscriberSpan`. | platform/pg reads options, state and hooks across the package boundary; there is no sqlc code yet, so `pg` defines the sqlc `DBTX` shape, and the pool must satisfy it without exposing pgxpool, whose `Acquire` waits as long as the caller's context allows ([pgx v5.11.0 pgxpool/pool.go](https://github.com/jackc/pgx/blob/v5.11.0/pgxpool/pool.go), retrieved 2026-10-13); `pg.Pool` bounds it with `database.acquire_timeout`. |
| M1-13/9 | 2026-10-12 | `Registry.SetTracer` may be called after `Seal`; `Publish` with an unsealed registry is an internal error. | The tracer changes only telemetry, never what runs; delivering before the list is complete would skip subscribers silently. |
| M1-13/10 | 2026-10-12 | `txn.RightsChange` names principals only; targets (space, channel) come with the authz hook of M1-29, which resolves the affected principals. | The brief fixes the signature `RightsChange(affected ...ids.ID)`; 02 "Flow" item 1 also allows a target, which needs the authz resolution this ticket does not build. |
| M1-13/11 | 2026-10-12 | `txn.Unit` is a struct of `kernel/txn`, not an interface `platform/pg` implements: `txn.NewUnit(reg, opts)` returns the unit and the only function that changes its phase; `platform/pg` keeps the transaction under its own context key and accepts it only together with the unit it created. `WithUnit` and `UnitFrom` take and return `*txn.Unit`. | The brief asked for an interface, but the deep scan of `make arch` (go-arch-lint) rejects passing a `platform/pg` implementation into `kernel` (`Dependency platform-pg -\-> kernel-ports not allowed`), and `kernel` depends on nothing (02 4.5). The phase setter stays with its creator, so code that reads the unit cannot fake the phase that `instance.Counters` (M1-15) checks. |
| M1-13/12 | 2026-10-12 | Findings of the red team (minors): `CheckTxnDirections` rejects a handler whose path holds more than one `/internal/`; the Semgrep rule `bothy-go-tx-only-in-platform-pg` also flags `Commit`, `Rollback`, `txn.NewUnit` and `txn.WithUnit` outside `internal/platform/pg`; the package documentation says that a module wrapper could still pass a foreign closure off as its own. A deadlock or lock timeout on a context that has already ended comes back as the raw database error, as the brief asks; M1-18 maps it. | The last-segment rule of M1-13/1 would otherwise let `internal/wire/internal/sync` act as sync in the product; `pg.Tx` hands out the whole `pgx.Tx`, and a commit or a unit built outside the unit of work would skip the hooks or fake the phase. |

### M1-14 Background jobs with River

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-14/1 | 2026-10-12 | The module of a job kind is the segment after the last `/internal/` of the argument type's package (`txn.ModuleOfPackage`); the tests use the stand-in module `internal/platform/jobs/internal/fakemodule`. `ValidateArgsType` also refuses unexported fields, which the brief does not list. | Same derivation as the txn registry (M1-13/1). An unexported field never reaches River's JSON, so the handler would see a zero value. |
| M1-14/2 | 2026-10-12 | `InsertTx` is allowed in the phases `rights_begin`, `work` and `rights_check`, and refuses arguments that do not survive River's JSON encoding (a zero `ids.ID` is no valid UUIDv7) with an internal error; `AddPeriodic` checks the same. | The brief forbids only `sync` and `audit`. Found in the first integration run: such arguments failed every attempt at run time; at the insert the caller's unit rolls back instead. |
| M1-14/3 | 2026-10-12 | A worker whose registry is empty does not start River; `Start` is a no-op then, as for `api`. | River v0.49.0 refuses to start a client without workers ("at least one Worker must be added to the Workers bundle", [client.go](https://github.com/riverqueue/river/blob/v0.49.0/client.go), retrieved 2026-10-13), and until M1-16 registers the first handler there is nothing to run. |
| M1-14/4 | 2026-10-12 | `internal/wire` registers two stop steps in phase `jobs`: the soft stop with budget `shutdown.jobs_soft_timeout` (counted in the budget sum), then `jobs_wait` with what `shutdown.total_timeout` leaves after the fixed budgets of the role (soft stop, telemetry flush, and for `all` drain and HTTP grace), left out when that is 0. `Runtime.Stop` cancels the running jobs at once when its context ends and waits again on a second call. | River escalates to the hard stop after `SoftStopTimeout`; a single step with exactly that budget would leave no time for the cancelled jobs to record their state before the pool closes, and their rows would wait an hour for River's rescuer. A budget of 0 (the whole rest) let a handler that ignores its cancelled context take the telemetry flush's budget (red team, minor). |
| M1-14/5 | 2026-10-12 | `TestShutdown_SoftTimeoutCancelsAndRetries` checks the row as `available` with attempt 0 and no recorded error after the stop, and the next run on attempt 1. | River v0.49.0 marks a job its own shutdown cancelled as interrupted: back to `available` without counting the attempt (`JobSetStateInterrupted` with `Attempt-1` for a soft-stopped job in [internal/jobexecutor/job_executor.go](https://github.com/riverqueue/river/blob/v0.49.0/internal/jobexecutor/job_executor.go), retrieved 2026-10-13); the brief expected `retryable` with attempt 1 and an error. The acceptance criterion (cancelled after the soft timeout, runs again) holds. |
| M1-14/6 | 2026-10-12 | The panic line `job panicked` carries `bothy.job.kind` and `error.type=panic`, not the job ID; the job row records `jobs: job <kind> panicked`, never the panic value. | The attribute catalogue (signals.md 5.3) has no job ID key, and the log handlers drop unknown keys; the line lies inside the job span, and the row with its attempt is the place to find the job. A panic value can carry content. |
| M1-14/7 | 2026-10-12 | River's nightly reindexer is off (`ReindexerSchedule = NeverSchedule`); all other River defaults of v0.49.0 stay (README of `platform/jobs`). | `bothy_app` owns no index of River, so `REINDEX` could only fail every night; reindexing stays with the owner role. |
| M1-14/8 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy migrate validate` stays without a database and checks River's embedded migrations (load, numbering); the database check `ValidateMigrations` runs at the start of `bothy serve` for every role, and `status` prints the River version. | 02 "Commands of the binary" says `validate` needs no database; River's `Validate` needs one. Checking at the start is the counterpart of the goose version check (R-AR-16). |
| M1-14/9 | 2026-10-12 | The schema step of `migrate up` also revokes `PUBLIC` on `jobs.schema`, grants on everything that already exists besides the default privileges, and runs under a session advisory lock; `jobs.schema` must not be `public` or `information_schema` or start with `pg_`; reserved words such as `user` stay allowed. | Idempotent also for tables an earlier run created; two concurrent `migrate up` must not race on `CREATE SCHEMA` (pg `Step` contract); River tables must never land in the domain schema (R-AR-09). |
| M1-14/10 | 2026-10-12 | API details beyond the brief: `New` takes `*pg.Pool`, and `pg.Pool` gains `PGX()` for River's driver; `jobs.Config` gains `Logger`; a nil tracer means the global provider's; `Migrate` returns what it applied; `lifecycle.Runner.FailOn` watches `Failed`. `jobs.queue.*.max_workers` are bounded from 1 to River's `QueueNumWorkersMax`. | River's pgx driver needs the pgx pool; the command prints what River applied; the lifecycle stays free of `platform/jobs`; `QueueNumWorkersMax` (10_000 in [client.go](https://github.com/riverqueue/river/blob/v0.49.0/client.go), retrieved 2026-10-13) is River's limit per queue. |
| M1-14/11 | 2026-10-12 | The work happens on branch `feat/M1-14-background-jobs` from `int/m1-wave3`; it is not merged locally into a base branch, the PR lane ports it. | Launcher task overrides the brief's local merge. |
| M1-14/12 | 2026-10-12 | `jobs.Migrate` and `jobs.New` for `worker` and `all` refuse a pool below two connections (`database.max_conns`) with an error naming the key. | The advisory lock of `Migrate` holds one connection while River migrates on another, and a working River client holds one for LISTEN; River waits for a connection without a deadline, so a pool of one hung until the caller's deadline (reproduced in `TestMigrateNeedsTwoConnections`). |
| M1-14/13 | 2026-10-12 | `TestQuerier_AcquireTimeout` (M1-13) takes its one held connection through `Pool.PGX()` instead of `Pool.Acquire`; a test-only change outside the brief's file list. | Under the load of `make check` the first dial took longer than the test's 50ms `database.acquire_timeout` and failed with `unavailable: the database is not reachable` before the test reached what it checks; the gate must be green for this ticket. |

Tests for the error paths of `Migrate`, `MigrationStatus` and `ValidateMigrations` (invalid schema, fresh schema, ended context, lock wait ended by the context) raise the coverage of the new code. | `Args` names what a value is, the arguments of a job, and is the name every job type and the brief use; the `-er` form is a convention of Effective Go ([Interface names](https://go.dev/doc/effective_go#interface-names), retrieved 2026-10-12). The analysis of pull request 30 found 76 percent coverage on new code against the gate's threshold. |

### M3-17 Notification decision

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-17-1 | 2026-10-12 | The decision per recipient lives in `services/server/internal/notify/domain` and is split into `ResolveLevel`, `InQuietHours`, `Decide`, `Flush` and `ReleaseDelayed`. They take facts and `Now` in and never call `authorize`, read a clock, load a time zone or read configuration: `Allowed` is the `push.deliver` result the caller gathered with current rights, each quiet-hour rule carries its loaded `*time.Location`, and the caller passes the values of `notify.coalesce_window`, `notify.mobile_delay_when_desktop_active` and `notify.user_default_level` and the personal switches resolved with their defaults. `TestDomainPurity` and `make arch` hold it. Place: the package, its README. | 13 12 point 3 and 02 5 "Layers within a module": the domain holds pure functions; the rules are proven by tables instead of by PostgreSQL, a clock or a provider, and M3-18, M3-19 and M3-23 call one rule set instead of re-implementing it. |
| D-M3-17-2 | 2026-10-12 | Recipient guards and kinds without thread: the author never gets `message`, `mention` or `dm` for their own message; `run_done`, `run_failed` and `input_needed` reach only the person who triggered the run (which makes "`input_needed` (own run)" of 13 4.3 hold); `security` and `admin` have no thread, so no level, no activity in a thread and no window apply to them (`ThreadID` must be zero, `Window` is ignored), quiet hours do. A `dm` notification requires a DM channel, and `message`, `mention` and the run kinds require a channel that is not one; a mismatch is invalid input. For `security` and `admin`, `Allowed` is the recipient rule of 13 2 as the caller evaluates it. Place: 13 2 and 4.4. | The domain does not classify events into kinds (M3-19 does), but a wrong caller must fail closed. A message in a DM channel is `dm` also when it mentions r, because both kinds need the same level and go at once; 05 4.9 defines `push.deliver` on a thread only. |
| D-M3-17-3 | 2026-10-12 | `muted_until` counts only on the chosen own row (thread, channel or space, the first present) and only while it is strictly after now; at now it has passed and the row's stored level applies again. A `muted_until` on a row that was not chosen has no effect; a row stored as `mute` stays `mute` after its `muted_until` passed. `EffectiveLevel` carries `Level`, `Source` (the step of 13 3.1) and `Muted`. Place: `level.go`. | 13 3.1 "If `muted_until` of the selected row lies in the future": "in the future" is strict; an inner row decides even when an outer one is muted, so a thread the person follows keeps its own level. |
| D-M3-17-4 | 2026-10-12 | Quiet hours: start and end are whole minutes (`HH:MM`, 0 to 1439), the wall time is compared to the second; `start_local = end_local` is a window of 24 hours from `start_local` on each set weekday (screen D9 "all day" is `00:00` to `00:00`); the caller loads the zone of the row or `users.time_zone`; every rule, also a disabled one, must be well formed; a delayed push keeps the `Quiet` flag of its decision. Place: 13 3.4, `quiet.go`. | The schema stores `HH:MM`; second precision makes 06:59:59 quiet and 07:00:00 not. 13 3.4 point 3 already covers `end_local <= start_local`, so equal times are a full day, not an empty window. The gateway copy of the same notification went out quietly at once, and the delay is at most `notify.mobile_delay_when_desktop_active`. A broken row fails closed instead of being skipped silently. |
| D-M3-17-5 | 2026-10-12 | Window rules: the window of (recipient, thread) is open while `Now < End` and closed at `Now == End`; a kind with thread that sends while no window is open opens one until `Now + notify.coalesce_window`, whatever its kind; `message` in an open window is collected; urgent kinds in an open window send and leave the window unchanged; a window length of zero opens none. `Flush` (kind `message` only) gives `nothing_collected` for nothing collected and `read` for an unread count of zero or less; otherwise allowed, level, activity, quiet and route run again at the flush time, `Author` and `Window` are ignored, and a send carries the unread count of 03 `read_states` and opens the next window; a flush that sends nothing leaves the window closed. Place: 13 4.5, `decide.go`, `flush.go`. | 13 1 point 3 "One notification per thread and window" must hold under a steady stream, and the first message after a quiet period must still go at once (R-PN-06). Rights, level and quiet hours may change during a window; a bundle has no single author. |
| D-M3-17-6 | 2026-10-12 | Activity and routes: a device is active in the thread with a connection that is not idle and whose focus holds the thread, on any platform, and that stops the notification on every device; the desktop is active with a `macos` or `windows` device connected and not idle. Routes follow the order of `Devices`: a connection (idle or not) gets `gateway`; otherwise a device with registrations gets `push_delayed` (`NotBefore = Now + notify.mobile_delay_when_desktop_active`) if it is `ios` or `android`, the desktop is active and the delay is above zero, else `push`; a device with neither is left out. No route at all gives `none` with `no_route` and opens no window. `ReleaseDelayed` drops a delayed push exactly when the read position (the greater of `last_read_seq` and `threads.unread_floor_seq`) reaches the notification's `thread_seq`; a notification without a message is always released. Place: 13 4.4 and 4.6, `decide.go`, `delay.go`. | 13 4.4 and 4.6 name the conditions; an idle desktop window still gets `notification.created` but does not count as someone watching. A notification that reaches no device must not open a window, or the next message would be collected and never shown. |
| D-M3-17-7 | 2026-10-12 | Invalid input: `ResolveLevel`, `InQuietHours`, `Decide` and `Flush` validate first and return `errs.New(errs.CodeInternalError, ...)` with the zero result (for `Decide` and `Flush` outcome `none`, no routes) for an unknown kind, level or platform; a zero `Now`; a negative window length or delay; a kind with thread and a zero `ThreadID`, or `security` or `admin` with one; an empty or unknown instance default for a kind with thread; the DM mismatch of D-M3-17-2; a zero or duplicate device ID, a zero registration ID or a zero thread ID in a focus; a malformed quiet-hour rule; for `Flush` a kind other than `message` or a negative `Collected`. The error text names the field, never a value. `Outcome` is an integer enum whose zero value is `none`, so the zero `Decision` never sends. Place: the package. | Fail closed: a broken input never yields `send` or `collect`. The caller built the facts from its own tables, so a broken fact is a server fault (`internal_error`), not a client error. |
| D-M3-17-8 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M3-17-notify-decision` from `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. Text added to chapter 13 and the notify README is English (the ticket was built on the German chapters and ported onto the English ones). The rows stand in a section per ticket like the M1 rulings, with the IDs the brief names. A test helper file `fixtures_test.go` holds the shared fixtures of the test files the brief lists. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's merge step and language; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |

### M1-18 HTTP middleware chain and problem details

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-18/1 | 2026-10-12 | The request validator is `github.com/pb33f/libopenapi-validator` v0.16.1 (MIT, OpenAPI 3.1, JSON Schema 2020-12: its [LICENSE](https://github.com/pb33f/libopenapi-validator/blob/v0.16.1/LICENSE) and [README](https://github.com/pb33f/libopenapi-validator/blob/v0.16.1/README.md), retrieved 2026-10-13) with `github.com/pb33f/libopenapi` v0.41.3. The probe tests `TestValidator_Probe_*` pass on the first library: `additionalProperties: false`, `unevaluatedProperties: false` on an `allOf` composition, `format: uuid` (with format assertions on), enums, `minLength`, `maxItems`, `oneOf` with discriminator, header and path parameters; kin-openapi was not needed. The validator keeps only the operations the chain mounts (the model is pruned before the schemas are compiled), and an operation it does not know fails closed with 500. | The brief names the library and its fallback. Compiling every request schema of the 29 000-line contract took about 3 s on the build machine, against 5 s of `perf.startup_ready_max`, for routes that are not served; the unmounted operations answer 404 at the mux anyway. |
| M1-18/2 | 2026-10-12 | `platform/httpserver` has its own `Operation` and `Route` types; `internal/wire` converts `routes.Operation` with `HTTPOperation`. The harness `httpservertest` builds the chain around these operations. The validator takes the contract as bytes; `api/rest.Spec` embeds `api/rest/openapi.gen.yaml`. | The module register gives `platform` no edge to `api` (the generated layer), and the harness lives in the `platform-httpserver` component; a copy of six fields keeps the platform package independent of the generator. A test (`TestHTTPOperationCarriesEveryField`) holds the conversion and the equal enum values. |
| M1-18/3 | 2026-10-12 | FieldError `code` vocabulary of schema validation: `required`, `unknown_member`, `invalid_type`, `invalid_format`, `invalid_enum`, `too_long`, `too_short`, `too_large`, `too_small`, `too_many_items`, `too_few_items`, `pattern_mismatch`, `invalid`; use cases keep `limit_reached` and `not_enabled` of 06. `pointer` names the member itself also for `required` and `unknown_member` (the validator reports those at the enclosing object; the chain appends the member name). `detail` is a fixed English text per code and never repeats the value. | The brief fixes the list; 06 section 5 item 3 already names `limit_reached` and `not_enabled`, so the contract description lists both sets. |
| M1-18/4 | 2026-10-12 | `If-Match: *` on an operation that requires If-Match is `428 precondition_required`, like a missing header. A weak tag, a list or a non-decimal value is not valid for the use case, which answers `412 version_conflict` with `current`. | The wildcard would skip the version check the contract demands; a strong comparison of a weak tag fails ([RFC 9110 section 13.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-13.1.1), retrieved 2026-10-13). |
| M1-18/5 | 2026-10-12 | Problem titles come from `x-problem-codes` of the schema `Problem` (`errs.Code.Title`, held by `TestTitlesMatchOpenAPI`), not from the title rule of the brief. | The rule (code with spaces, first letter upper case) disagrees with the contract for `cursor_invalid` ("Invalid cursor"), `use_dpop_nonce`, `invalid_dpop_proof`, `step_up_required`, `channel_read_only`, `title_manual`, `idempotency_key_reuse`, `rate_limited` and `unavailable`; R-API-23 makes `x-problem-codes` the one source of titles. |
| M1-18/6 | 2026-10-12 | Null repair: the response writer buffers `application/json` and `application/problem+json` bodies, removes object members whose value is `null` and logs `response contained JSON null` with `http.route` and `error.type = null_member`; a `null` array element, a top-level `null`, an integer beyond `maxSafeInteger` (the JSON safe-integer limit, `sanitize.go`) or a body that is not one JSON value replaces the response by `500 internal_error` (`response refused`). Clean bodies go out byte for byte. The harness fails every handler test that triggers the repair. The JSON Pointer of the removed member is not logged. | ADR 0053 and 06 R-API-03. The log allowlist of M1-10 has no key for a JSON Pointer, and adding one needs signals.md and the collector allowlist (M1-10 scope); the route and the harness failure locate the handler. |
| M1-18/7 | 2026-10-12 | Unmatched requests (404, 405) carry no `instance`. | `instance` is optional in RFC 9457 and in the schema `Problem`; without a route the server cannot know which segments are secret (`{token}` of `postIncomingWebhook` is not mounted yet, and a later route could add another), and echoing a raw path could put a webhook token into a response (SEC-LOG-3). Matched routes write the path with every `{token}` segment of the template kept as `{token}`. |
| M1-18/8 | 2026-10-12 | A body on an operation without request body is `415 unsupported_media_type`; a missing required body is `400 validation_failed` with `errors[]` `{pointer: "", code: required}`. Multipart and octet-stream bodies are not read by the chain: their limit (`file.max_upload_bytes`) and transfer deadline apply while the handler streams, and their parameters are validated. | SEC-IN-1 rejects what the contract does not describe; reading an upload into memory would defeat the stream limit of SEC-IN-3. |
| M1-18/9 | 2026-10-12 | The recover step logs `panic recovered` with `error.type = panic`, the correlation ID and `code.function.name` of the function that panicked first; the stack itself is not logged. A panic after the response started becomes `http.ErrAbortHandler`. | 02 says "stack only in the log", but the allowlist of M1-10 and the collector drop `exception.stacktrace` on purpose; the function name locates the panic without new keys. |
| M1-18/10 | 2026-10-12 | The proof applies to every success of a route, also to an empty response (implicit 200), to a protocol switch (Hijack is refused without a check) and to a success produced by a stage before `StageProof` (a slot of a later ticket): without a check of the route's action it becomes `500 internal_error`. M1-20 replays a stored response only after recording the check of its action. | Fail closed (B7, R-AR-14): no code path may answer a success that no `authorize` call allowed. |
| M1-18/11 | 2026-10-12 | `server.public_url` enters the schema as a required key of kind `url`, with the cross-check `public_url_origin` (origin form, as 16 asks of `bothy config check`); `api.cors_allowed_origins` gets `cors_origins`. Every command that loads the server configuration (`serve`, `migrate`, `config`, `telemetry`) therefore needs `server.public_url`; Compose sets it for api, worker and migrate (`x-server-env`), and the tests set `https://chat.example.com`. | 16 "Configuration values of this chapter" says "no default, required"; the DPoP htu of StageAuthenticate is built from it. One schema serves all commands (M1-07). |
| M1-18/12 | 2026-10-12 | W7 closes every object schema reachable from request bodies of operations under `paths` with `additionalProperties: false`; maps (`additionalProperties` with a schema) and `JsonObject` stay open; webhooks under `webhooks:` are not walked. The generator check `request schemas closed` (`api/routes/gen/closed.go`) fails `make codegen-check` on a new open request schema and names it; a schema composed with `allOf` must carry `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Schemas shared with responses or events are closed too. | The brief's rule; webhooks are requests the server sends, where R-API-04 keeps additivity. A shared schema cannot be both; SEC-IN-1 wins for the request (W7), and clients keep ignoring unknown members in responses by the tolerance rule of 06 (G5). |

Section references to German headings are replaced by the English headings. The request schemas that main gained after the ticket was built (`HostReportedSessionUser`, `HostSessionUserAssign`, `InvocationTokenRenew`) are closed as W7 requires. `TestConfigKeysMatchChapterTables` of M1-07 reads the same term `no default, required`, and `TestJobsConfigBindsDefaults` of M1-14 sets the now required `server.public_url`. | With this ticket the key enters the schema, so D5 compares the cell for the first time and `make config-doc-check` failed on the old wording. |

M1-18 adds the packages that make the collision likely; M1-15 alone passed 9 of 9 runs. The race fails every test that runs at the same time and `make test` runs with `-race`, so CI would fail at random. After the change 10 of 10 runs passed. Raising the Go pin is not the integration's decision. |

The writer lives for one request, so its context never outlives it. |

### M1-23 Server image

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-23/1 | 2026-10-12 | The final stage of `bothy-server` is `gcr.io/distroless/static-debian13:nonroot`, pinned by the digest of its image index, not `scratch`. Debian 13 is the newest line the distroless project lists as supported at build time ("Distroless images are based on Debian 13 (trixie)", [distroless README](https://github.com/GoogleContainerTools/distroless), retrieved 2026-10-13). | Distroless static brings maintained CA certificates, time zone data and an `/etc/passwd` entry for `nonroot`, which a static Go binary needs for outgoing TLS and local time, without a shell or package manager ("distroless images by default do not contain a shell", same README). Its digest is a normal Docker dependency that Renovate (`config:best-practices`) keeps current; with `scratch` both files would have to be copied and updated by hand. |
| M1-23/2 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M1-23-server-image` from the head of `int/m1-wave3` (`ca30b63`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the boundaries of the brief for base branch and merge. |
| M1-23/3 | 2026-10-12 | The build argument `VERSION` in `release.yml` comes from `needs.resolve.outputs.version`, like the other steps of the job `build` on this base. On a base whose job `build` resolves the tag again (step `info`, D-W-52 on `main`), the port uses `steps.info.outputs.version`, as the brief asks. | The base `int/m1-wave3` has neither the step `info` nor the rule `release-untrusted-output`; a reference to a missing step would be empty and fail the build on the version check. The Dockerfile refuses a version that differs from `services/server/VERSION` either way. |
| M1-23/4 | 2026-10-12 | `TestReadOnlyRunAsNonRoot` passes `BOTHY_DATABASE__HOST=postgres` instead of `BOTHY_SERVER__PUBLIC_URL`. | `bothy config check` needs no variable on this base, and `server.public_url` is not yet a key of the schema: the loader rejects the variable as unknown. One known key still proves that the environment reaches the loader in the hardened container. |
| M1-23/5 | 2026-10-12 | The image contains the empty directory `/var/lib/bothy/signing-keys` (default of `secrets.signing_key_dir`) with owner `65532:65532` and mode `0700`, created by `COPY --from=build --chown --chmod`, not by `RUN` in the final stage; `TestSigningKeyDir` checks it. | 18 "Server images" rule 3 requires it, and a named volume takes owner and mode from the image on first mount (an empty volume mounted over existing files gets them "propagated (copied) into the volume", [Docker volumes, Mounting a volume over existing data](https://docs.docker.com/engine/storage/volumes/#mounting-a-volume-over-existing-data), retrieved 2026-10-13), so the server can write its keys without an init step. A `RUN` in the final stage would need a shell and emulation. |
| M1-23/6 | 2026-10-12 | The `HEALTHCHECK` of the image carries the timings of `x-server-healthcheck` too (interval `15s`, timeout `5s`, start period `30s`, `4` retries); `TestHealthcheckMatchesCompose` compares command and timings with `infra/compose/compose.yaml`. | A runtime without Compose then probes the same way, and the acceptance criterion "the health check of the image equals the Compose health check" is held by a test instead of by review. |
| M1-23/7 | 2026-10-12 | The pin check treats a `FROM` whose reference is built from a variable as a finding unless the digest is written out; stage names compare case-insensitively; only the leading parser directive block counts for `# syntax=`; a comment inside a continued instruction is skipped. Two repository tests hold the golang tag of the builder equal to the `toolchain` of `services/server/go.mod` and the base images in the server README equal to the Dockerfile. | A variable cannot be verified statically. BuildKit reads directives only before the first comment, blank line or instruction, so a later `# syntax=` is a plain comment ("Once a comment, empty line or builder instruction has been processed, BuildKit no longer looks for parser directives", [Dockerfile reference, Parser directives](https://docs.docker.com/reference/dockerfile/#parser-directives), retrieved 2026-10-13). Renovate could bump the golang image without the toolchain, and the README table is a number in documentation that a test must hold. |
| M1-23/8 | 2026-10-12 | The image tests read `BOTHY_TEST_IMAGE` and `BOTHY_TEST_IMAGE_OCI` through one helper with a `//nolint:forbidigo` and run Docker through one helper with a `//nolint:gosec` (G204), each with its reason; `.golangci.yml` is unchanged. Containers start with `--pull=never`. | The suppressions stay at the two places that need them, without widening a path rule. `--pull=never` keeps the tests offline and makes a missing local image fail instead of pulling a namesake from a registry. |
| M1-23/9 | 2026-10-12 | The row `docker/setup-qemu-action` leaves `docs/quality/toolchain.md`, because `release.yml` no longer uses the action. Text added by this ticket to German documents (18-release.md, gates.md, the server README) is English. | A pinned action without a user is documentation drift. The launcher requires English for everything added; M1-11/12 set the precedent of English text in German chapters during the translation. |
| M1-23/10 | 2026-10-12 | Red team (verdict NEIN, one Major, three Minors), all fixed: the cleanup of the image tests removes containers under a context without the cancellation of `t.Context()`, which Go cancels before cleanup functions run, and logs a failed removal; `TestCleanupRemovesContainers` proves that no container outlives its test. The pin check also checks the images of `COPY --from=` and `RUN --mount=...,from=` (a stage name or index passes) and finds Dockerfiles by name in any case (`Dockerfile`, `Containerfile`, with a prefix or suffix after a dot). `TestImageConfig` asserts that the image exposes no port. | The Major was reproduced: every `make image-test` left three containers in state `created`. SEC-SC-4 covers every image that enters a build, not only `FROM`, and a lower-case `build.dockerfile` would have escaped the gate. |
| M1-23/11 | 2026-10-12 | `TestQuerier_AcquireTimeout` (M1-13, `internal/platform/pg/unit_test.go`) retries its first `Acquire` for up to ten seconds before it holds the only connection; the three checks on the exhausted pool are unchanged. This test file is outside the scope of the brief. | The full `make check` of this ticket failed twice in three runs on this test with "the database is not reachable": the first connection is dialed under the 50 ms acquire timeout, and under the load of `make check` the dial takes longer. With `-cpu 1` it failed one run in four before the change and passed six of six after it. The pool keeps a connection whose acquire gave up, so the retry gets it. Leaving it would keep `make check` red for every ticket on this base. |
| M1-23/12 | 2026-10-12 | Port onto main: the build argument `VERSION` in `release.yml` comes from `steps.info.outputs.version`, as M1-23/3 foresaw for a base with the step `info`. `TestReadOnlyRunAsNonRoot` passes `BOTHY_SERVER__PUBLIC_URL=https://bothy.example.com` instead of `BOTHY_DATABASE__HOST=postgres`, which supersedes M1-23/4. `TestQuerier_AcquireTimeout` keeps the form of M1-14 (M1-14/13: the held connection through `Pool.PGX()` without the acquire timeout) instead of the retry loop of M1-23/11. Section references name the English headings of main. | M1-18 on main makes `server.public_url` a required key for every command that loads the configuration (M1-18/11), so the image config check failed with `server.public_url: is required`; the required key still proves that the environment reaches the loader in the hardened container. M1-14 and M1-23 fixed the same flake; one fix for one cause, and the M1-14 form does not depend on a retry window. |

### M1-21 Transport protection

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-21/1 | 2026-10-12 | A `secret_ref` key with an empty default (chapter cell "empty": `server.tls_cert_ref`, `server.tls_key_ref`) is optional: the empty text, as default or from a source, is the value "no reference" and switches its feature off; `bothy config check --secrets` prints `not set` for it and reads nothing. A `secret_ref` key with a reference as default still refuses the empty text. This replaces the rule "no empty `secret_ref` default" of M1-07/11, as that ruling foresaw. | 16 "5.2 Secret provider (ADR 0011)" names "empty" for secrets of a feature an operator can switch off; a separate flag per feature would be a second switch for the same fact. |
| M1-21/2 | 2026-10-12 | The cross-checks `tls_pair` (both TLS references or neither), `tls_https` (TLS needs an `https` `server.public_url`) and `hsts_whole_seconds` (`http.hsts_max_age` a multiple of 1s; `Min` `0s` refuses negative values) live in `internal/wire` with the keys, so their tests are in `internal/wire` and not in `internal/platform/config`, which has no key of its own. `httpserver.ChainConfig` checks `http.hsts_max_age` once more for callers that bypass the loader. | The keys and the cross-checks of the server are registered in `internal/wire`; a kind rule "whole seconds" for one key would add a field to `config.Key` for a single user. |
| M1-21/3 | 2026-10-12 | `httpserver` may use `platform/secrets` (module register): `TransportConfig` holds `secrets.Ref`, and the provider comes in through the narrow interface `httpserver.SecretGetter` (`Get`), which `*secrets.Provider` satisfies. `platform/secrets` stays unchanged. | The brief fixes the reference type of `platform/secrets`; that package has no provider interface, and adding one there is outside the scope of this ticket. |
| M1-21/4 | 2026-10-12 | The TLS material is checked at start step 2 by `httpserver.CheckTransport` and loaded again by `ListenPublic` at step 8 (the provider cache makes the second read free within `secrets.cache_ttl`). Only `api` and `all` check it; the worker opens no public listener and never reads the TLS references. | Bad material must end the start before the database wait (02 "15.1 Start"); a worker container need not mount the certificate. |
| M1-21/5 | 2026-10-12 | A rotated pair goes through the same checks as at start (PEM chain, matching key, validity, host of `server.public_url`). A failed pair of versions keeps the previous certificate and logs `TLS certificate rotation failed, keeping the previous certificate` (`error.type` `tls_rotation`) once; it is parsed again at most once per `rotationRetry` (`tlscert.go`), so a pair refused for a reason that passes with time (a leaf not yet valid for the server clock) is picked up without a restart. A good pair clears the memory of failed pairs. `GetCertificate` holds its lock over read and swap, so an older pair cannot replace a newer one. A rotation that replaces the two files at different times can log one such line until both are read again. `TransportConfig.Clock` (nil: system clock) drives validity and retry. | 02 "14.4": a failed rotation keeps the old value; one line per version, not per handshake, and at most one parse per minute for a broken file. |
| M1-21/6 | 2026-10-12 | The refusal log has constant messages and names the listener in `bothy.reason` (value `public`, from the fixed list of listener names); the first refusal per listener has the hint to add the proxy to `server.trusted_proxies` (Warn), later ones only `plaintext connection from an untrusted peer refused` (Debug). | The log allowlist of `platform/otel` keeps only catalog keys and the catalog has no listener key; `sloglint` requires constant messages (its settings in `services/server/.golangci.yml`, after docs/observability/signals.md "Logs"). |
| M1-21/7 | 2026-10-12 | SEC-HDR-1 (with HSTS) is set in the outermost step before the next handler runs, and a secure writer applies it once more right before the header block goes out, so no handler can drop `nosniff`, weaken HSTS or send `Server`. The internal listener uses the same writer through `InternalHandler`, which also sets `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` (health answers JSON) and answers unknown paths and methods with problem bodies; health registers `GET` patterns, which also match `HEAD` ("A pattern with the method GET matches both GET and HEAD requests", [net/http ServeMux](https://pkg.go.dev/net/http#ServeMux), retrieved 2026-10-13). | Design note 7 asks that inner responses inherit the headers; applying them at write time closes the gap of a handler that changes them. |
| M1-21/8 | 2026-10-12 | The TLS listener is an own wrapper around the TCP listener with `tls.Server` per connection and `NextProtos` `h2`, `http/1.1`, the list `net/http` uses for `ServeTLS` (`adjustNextProtos` in [net/http server.go of Go 1.27.2](https://github.com/golang/go/blob/go1.27.2/src/net/http/server.go), retrieved 2026-10-13); `http.Server.Serve` then serves HTTP/2 on connections that negotiated it. | The brief has `ListenPublic` return the listener; the wrapper keeps the TLS configuration inspectable for SEC-T17 (no own cipher list). |
| M1-21/9 | 2026-10-12 | The SEC-T03 row "503 while shutting down" on the public listener is the `503 unavailable` problem of the shared writer: the public listener has no own shutdown answer (readiness turns false, the drain waits, then `Shutdown` closes the listener). The shutdown state on the internal listener is `/readyz` `503` with `shutting_down`, covered by the row "readyz starting" (same writer) and by `TestServeReadyzDuringDrain`. | Nothing in M1 answers 503 because of the shutdown on the public listener; a test row for a path that does not exist would prove nothing. |
| M1-21/10 | 2026-10-12 | Not covered by SEC-HDR-1: responses that `net/http` writes itself outside any handler (`400` for a malformed request line, `431` for too large headers, the plain answer to plaintext on the TLS port; all in `conn.serve` of [net/http server.go of Go 1.27.2](https://github.com/golang/go/blob/go1.27.2/src/net/http/server.go), retrieved 2026-10-13). The `429` row of SEC-T03 waits for M1-19 (`internal/platform/ratelimit` is not on the base). | Those answers are written by the standard library before the handler chain exists; they carry no body of the API. |

This replaces the merge step of the brief's definition of done. | Instruction of the launcher, which takes precedence over the brief. |

### M1-24 Audit chain, recorder and aggregation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-24/1 | 2026-10-12 | Canonical form of an IP address for `ip_hmac`: the client IP of `kernel/reqmeta` after `netip.Addr.Unmap()` and without zone, in the text form of `netip.Addr.String()` (IPv4 dotted decimal, IPv6 compressed lower case per [RFC 5952](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5952), retrieved 2026-10-13, as [netip.Addr.String](https://pkg.go.dev/net/netip#Addr.String) documents, retrieved 2026-10-13); `ip_hmac = hex(HMAC-SHA-256(key, text))` in full length (the hex form of the whole HMAC). Every entry of a request that carries a client IP gets `ip_hmac` and `ip_key_id`, also for an authenticated actor. | One text per address, so `::ffff:192.0.2.44` and `192.0.2.44` and an address with zone give one pseudonym (`TestIPHMAC_MappedEqualsIPv4`, `TestIPHMAC_ZoneIgnored`); the full length keeps the owner's check of a known address unambiguous within the retention (20 "4.5"). |
| M1-24/2 | 2026-10-12 | The closed reason list of 07 "4.1" lives in `internal/kernel/auditing` (`Reason`, one constant per row in table order, `KnownReasons`, `Reason.Known`); `authz` reuses it with M1-28. `TestReasonsMatchChapter07` parses the table and compares both ways and the order. | R-DM-28: one list, held closed by a test; the kernel port is the only package both `audit` and `authz` may import. |
| M1-24/3 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave4` at `76b79a5`, branch `feat/M1-24-audit-chain` in an own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in `internal/audit/README.md` (the brief asks for German); its German headings stay, because the module README layout is shared by all modules. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language. |
| M1-24/4 | 2026-10-12 | `kernel/txn` gets `Detach(ctx)`: a copy of ctx outside the unit of work with every other value. `RecordDetached`, the aggregator and the keyring run `UnitOfWork.Do` on it and so begin an own transaction even when the caller is inside one. The per-unit state the hook needs (`Key`, `Load`, `Store`) already existed. | The brief requires an own unit that never joins an ambient one; `platform/pg` joins whenever `txn.InUnit(ctx)` holds, and hiding the unit is the smallest change that keeps actor, correlation ID and client IP. |
| M1-24/5 | 2026-10-12 | `Attrs` refuses `ip_hmac`, `ip_key_id` and `aggregate` (the recorder sets them); `reason` takes only `auditing.ReasonText` and `violations` only `auditing.Violations`, and these two values are refused under any other key. `ReasonText` is checked for emptiness and with `kernel/textcheck`; its length bound `audit.reason_max_chars` stays with the request validation of the action, which answers `400 validation_failed` with the field code `too_long` (SEC-AUD-5), so the key is not in the schema yet. Codes and attribute keys are at most 64 bytes (`domain.MaxCodeLen`). | The brief's comment on `Attrs` names `violations` as reserved while its test list accepts `violations`; typed values under their reserved key satisfy both. A recorder that truncated or refused a long reason after the change would lose the entry or fail a valid action. |
| M1-24/6 | 2026-10-12 | The flush of the aggregation is the stop step `audit_denials` in `lifecycle.PhaseDatabase`, registered by the start step `audit` after `register` added the step `pool`; steps of one phase run in reverse registration order, so it runs before the pool closes in every role. `TestAudit_StartFlushesBeforeThePoolCloses` holds the order. No new lifecycle phase. | `lifecycle` has no audit phase and is outside the scope; the database phase is the last one that still has the pool. |
| M1-24/7 | 2026-10-12 | The module reports through the port `app.Reporter`, implemented by `adapters/logs` with constant messages and the keys `error.type`, `bothy.action`, `bothy.reason` and `db.response.status_code` (audit adapters may use pgx for the SQLSTATE). The count of dropped denials and the number of a missing row are not logged, and the principal of a dropped bucket is not logged either. | The app layer may not import `platform/otel/attr`; the attribute catalogue has no key for a count or a sequence number (adding one needs signals.md and the collector allowlist), and SEC-LOG-4 allows people in logs only as pseudonyms, which `platform/otel` derives and the app layer cannot reach. |
| M1-24/8 | 2026-10-12 | `Record` computes the IP pseudonym when it is called (work phase), not in the hook; the keyring reads and creates key rows only in its own transactions. A denial whose key is unavailable is aggregated without IP fields and reported; `Record` and `RecordDetached` fail instead. A correlation ID that is not a UUID is left out of the row. | A key row created in the caller's transaction would stay cached after a rollback while missing from the table; in the hook the second connection would be taken while holding `audit_seq`. `RecordDenial` must never return a storage error; losing the pseudonym is better than losing the denial. |
| M1-24/9 | 2026-10-12 | The queries of the brief get one more, `Rows` (rows `from_seq .. to_seq` in chain order, details decoded with `UseNumber`), and the hook inserts with one `INSERT ... SELECT` over parallel `unnest` arrays. | The chain tests recompute every hash from the row read back; M1-50 needs the same read. sqlc does not resolve the multi-argument `unnest` in `FROM` (observed with the pinned sqlc on 2026-10-12); set-returning functions in one select list run in lock step ([PostgreSQL, set-returning functions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/xfunc-sql.html#XFUNC-SQL-FUNCTIONS-RETURNING-SET), retrieved 2026-10-13). |
| M1-24/10 | 2026-10-12 | After a failed write of aggregated denials the timer waits one `audit.deny_aggregate_window` before the next attempt; `Record` in the audit phase itself is an error. The rules of `privacy.audit_ip_key_period`, `privacy.audit_ip_key_retention` and `privacy.audit_ip_kek_ref` leave `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml`, because the keys entered the schema (rule D6). | A failed bucket is due at once again; retrying without a pause would spin while the database is down. An entry recorded after the hook ran would never be written. |
| M1-24/11 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (verdict JA, eight Minors). Fixed: the sqlc entry of `audit` writes `*.gen.go` files (M1-05/7); concurrent `Record` calls in one unit of work append under one lock, so no entry is lost (`TestRecord_ConcurrentInOneUnitKeepsEveryEntry`); a string attribute that parses as an IP address is refused although it matches the code pattern (K-DP-2); the final flush at shutdown (`Aggregator.Close`) reports every bucket it could not write with `DenialsDropped`. Not changed: a batch whose COMMIT succeeded but whose acknowledgement was lost is retried with the same row ids and fails on `audit_events_id_key` until the cap drops it (new ids would count the denials twice; the case needs a lost acknowledgement); the pool pressure of the keyring at a period change and the synchronous write of an evicted bucket are named in the README limits and follow from 20 "4.5" and SEC-AUD-6; the count in the drop log is M1-24/7. The identity entry of `sqlc.yaml` keeps its file names, because it is outside this ticket. | The owner rule lists Minors once; the four fixes were small and each has a test. |
| M1-24/12 | 2026-10-12 | Port onto main: references name the English chapter files and headings of main; the subsection "Tables" in the code description of the audit README is "Tables and grants", because the English README already has the section "Tables" that `make register-check` reads and a second one is a finding; the three `privacy.audit_ip_*` rules leave `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml`; non-ASCII test strings stay, they test UTF-8 handling, written without umlauts (for example `Révision`) so the language gate passes; the golden chain `testdata/chain_v1.json` keeps its independently computed vector and stands on the allow list of the language gate. The reason list gains `terminal_no_session_user` (SR12), which chapter 07 section 4.1 on main lists after `i6_violation`; `TestReasonsMatchChapter07` holds the order. | Translation and conflict resolution only; the behaviour equals the approved ticket. |

An update that changes nothing passes. The adapter maps the trigger to `validation_failed` on `/id`. | Comparing the whole row makes a column that a later migration adds immutable by default instead of silently mutable. A no-op is no change. The repository never reaches the trigger (its revocation filters `revoked_at IS NULL`), so the mapping only covers a programming error, and the brief maps trigger violations to `validation_failed`. |
| M1-27/6 | 2026-10-12 | Repository writes store the times the caller passes (`created_at`, `added_at`, `granted_at`, `deleted_at`, `removed_at`, `revoked_at`) and never fall back to the column default `now()`; a zero time is kind internal. | One clock decides validity (brief "Outgoing port"); a default would mix the database clock with the server clock of `authorize`. |
