| M1-27/8 | 2026-10-12 | Names: the checks on `groups` are `groups_source_check`, `groups_provider_shape_check` and `groups_external_ref_shape_check`; the indexes are `groups_live_name_key`, `groups_claim_key`, `group_memberships_active_key`, `group_memberships_active_user_idx`, `group_memberships_expiry_idx`, `grants_active_<column>_idx` and `grants_expiry_idx`. A host grant to an agent violates both `grants_bot_limits_check` and `grants_host_shape_check`; PostgreSQL reports the first by name ("When a table has multiple CHECK constraints, they will be tested for each row in alphabetical order by name", [CREATE TABLE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-createtable.html), retrieved 2026-10-13), and both map to a client error. | The adapter maps by name, so the names are part of the contract and `TestAuthzGroupsGrantsSchema` holds them. |
| M1-27/9 | 2026-10-12 | Additions beyond the sketched API: `CapsOf`, `Capability.String`, `Grant.Shape` and `MembershipWithGroup` in `domain`. `CheckRevoke` does not apply rule 1 of `CheckCreate`. | They keep the use cases of M1-28, M1-29 and M1-48 from rebuilding sets and shapes by hand. Rule 1 judges a grant that the database check never lets exist, and an owner must be able to revoke anything (brief: "owner always allowed"). |
| M1-27/10 | 2026-10-12 | Port onto main, after M1-24: both tickets added migration version 4, so the migration of M1-27 becomes `00005_authz_groups_grants.sql` with unchanged SQL; `migrations.sha256`, the authz README, the server CHANGELOG and `authzVersion` in `migrations/authz_groups_grants_test.go` follow. `capNames` (`caps.go`) and `levelNames` (`levels.go`) carry a `//nolint:goconst` with its reason, because goconst counts per package and the generated catalogue metadata of M1-25 in the same package repeats `manage` and `read`. References name the English chapter files and headings of main (comment headers of the queries and the migration, so the checksum is recorded anew). | goose refuses a set with a duplicate version, and generated files are only excluded from the goconst report, not from its count (both observed on the stack on 2026-10-12). |

### M1-19 Rate limits per class

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-19/1 | 2026-10-12 | Rounding of the headers: `r` = whole tokens left after the decision, rounded down; `t` = seconds until full, rounded up, `0` when full; `Retry-After` = seconds until one token, rounded up and at least `1`. Tokens are fixed point (1/60e9 of a token, refilled by `per_minute` units per nanosecond), so every value is an exact integer division. Recorded in 06 "Rate limits" point 5. | Rounding down `r` and up `t` and `Retry-After` never promises a token that is not there; integer arithmetic makes the exact header tests possible without float drift. |
| M1-19/2 | 2026-10-12 | A principal, agent or host class counts an anonymous request (or one without an actor) by its client IP with the same normalisation as `rl.public_auth` (IPv6 by /64, IPv4-mapped unmapped). Keys carry a kind prefix (`ip:`, `p:`, `a:`, `h:`, `d:`, `t:`, `c:`, `w:`), and the internal key is `<class>\|<key>`. Recorded in 06 "Rate limits" point 6. | Unauthenticated calls must neither share one bucket (one client could starve all) nor fall into a principal's bucket; the prefixes keep the IP fallback and the principal apart within a class. |
| M1-19/3 | 2026-10-12 | `rl.webhook_in` is keyed by the path value `webhook_id` of `POST /api/v1/hooks/{webhook_id}/{token}`, never by the token; the start check refuses an operation of this class without `{webhook_id}` in its path. | 05 counts per incoming webhook; the ID names it, while the token is a secret that must not sit in memory as a map key or show up in a key derivation. Someone who knows a webhook ID can exhaust its bucket before the token check; that is the price of counting before the use case and matches the purpose of the class (bounding a flood on one webhook). |
| M1-19/4 | 2026-10-12 | Eviction: at most `ratelimit.max_tracked_keys` buckets in a fixed number of shards (at least `minPerShard` per shard, at most `maxShards` shards, `limiter.go`; shard by `hash/maphash` of the internal key with a random seed per limiter, instead of the FNV-1a the brief names, which a client can compute: [hash/maphash](https://pkg.go.dev/hash/maphash) "Each Seed value is local to a single process and cannot be serialized or otherwise recreated in a different process", retrieved 2026-10-13); the per-shard cap is the cap divided by the shards, rounded down, so the total never exceeds the key. An insert into a full shard removes every full bucket of a random sample of `evictSample` (Go map iteration order, which [the Go specification](https://go.dev/ref/spec#For_range) leaves unspecified, retrieved 2026-10-13), else the sampled bucket with the oldest last use. `Sweep` removes full buckets every `ratelimit.sweep_interval`. The limiter never denies because the table is full. Every REST key has a bounded length (IP, prefix or ID; a `webhook_id` that is not an ID is `404` before a bucket exists). Residual risk, accepted: one client that can create many distinct keys within one refill window (many `webhook_id` values or many IPv6 /64 prefixes) can make the limiter evict non-full buckets, its own included, and so reset them; the random seed means it has to flood every shard instead of one, that is more than `ratelimit.max_tracked_keys` fresh keys per refill window. | A full bucket decides like a missing one, so removing it changes nothing; the cap must survive a flood of keys without unbounded memory, and a limiter that denied on a full table would let that flood lock out every client. The seed and the key length check follow the red team of M1-19 (finding 2); a shared table per client would need a second limit layer that 05 does not define. |
| M1-19/5 | 2026-10-12 | The failed-credentials rule needs a check before the `Authenticator`, which the hook of M1-18 (called only after a failure) cannot give. `platform/httpserver` gets `Chain.SetCredentialsPrecheck` (runs only for requests with an `Authorization` header); its function returns a settle function that the stage calls with the result of the `Authenticator`. `ratelimit.Install` sets only the precheck; `SetFailedCredentialsHook` of M1-18 stays for other uses. The precheck reserves one token of `rl.public_auth` with `Allow`; an empty bucket is `429` with `RateLimit-Policy` and `RateLimit` of `rl.public_auth`. Settle keeps the token for a failure with `401` other than `token_expired` and `use_dpop_nonce` and returns it with `Limiter.Refund` otherwise (success, those two codes, an error of another kind such as `503` of the identity store). At most `burst` verifications of one client IP run per refill; more than `burst` requests with credentials of one client IP in flight at the same instant get `429` even with valid credentials. | 02 "Chain in the api node" bounds the signature checks a failing client can cause; a precheck that only looked let concurrent requests all pass on one token (red team M1-19, finding 1). A server failure is not the client's fault and must not lock out an IP; returning the token keeps valid credentials free of cost. The in-flight limit is the price of the bound and is far above a normal client's parallelism at the default burst. |
| M1-19/6 | 2026-10-12 | Files of `platform/httpserver` outside the brief's scope change: `Operation.RateLimitClass` (copied by `wire.HTTPOperation`, held by `TestHTTPOperationCarriesEveryField`), `SetCredentialsPrecheck`, and `httpservertest.Options.Setup`, which runs on the chain before the routes are mounted so tests install the limiter as `internal/wire` does. `platform/ratelimit` depends on `httpserver` and `otel` in `architecture/modules.yaml`. | The stage must read the class of the route from the context, and `routes.Operation` cannot be imported by the platform layer (M1-18/2). The harness had no way to set hooks before `Wrap` seals the chain. |
| M1-19/7 | 2026-10-12 | The key kind table is hand-written in `ratelimit/classes.go`, because `actions.yaml` holds the key column only as prose; `TestKeyKinds_MatchChapter05` parses the generated table of 05 and maps each wording to a kind. The kind list of the brief gets `KeyHost` for `rl.host` ("host"); `rl.terminal` and `rl.import` are principal classes as the table says (the person, the owner). | The brief allows the hand-written table with this test; without `KeyHost` the class of the host service would have no key. |
| M1-19/8 | 2026-10-12 | Bounds of the keys: `ratelimit.<class>.per_minute` and `.burst` from 1 to 1000000 (`ratelimit.MaxLimit`), `ratelimit.max_tracked_keys` and `ratelimit.sweep_interval` within the bounds of their schema entries (`configkeys_ratelimit.go`). A value outside stops the start with exit code 2 (configuration error); `ratelimit.New` checks the same bounds again. | Zero would disable a class or the sweeper (a ticker needs a positive interval); the upper bound keeps `burst` x 60e9 far inside int64. |
| M1-19/9 | 2026-10-12 | The middleware fails closed: an operation without a class, with an unknown class, with a gateway class (`rl.typing`, `rl.gateway_connect`) or without a key answers `500 internal_error` and never reaches the handler; the start check of `internal/wire` (`InstallRateLimits`) refuses these operations before the listener opens. `bothy.ratelimit.rejected` counts every denial of `Allow` (REST, gateway, failed credentials) and every refusal of the precheck. | Serving an operation without a limit would bypass the requirement silently. One counting point keeps the metric complete for the gateway tickets. |
| M1-19/10 | 2026-10-12 | Responses written before `StageRateLimit` (a `401` of `StageAuthenticate`, the precheck `429` aside) carry no `RateLimit` headers of the route's class. | The order of 02 "Chain in the api node" puts authentication before the rate limit; the class key of a principal is known only afterwards. |
| M1-19/11 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M1-19-rate-limits` from `int/m1-wave4` (`76b79a5`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. Text added to the German chapter 06 is English. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's merge step; everything added is English (M1-23/9). |
| M1-19/12 | 2026-10-12 | A `webhook_id` that is not an ID (06: UUIDv7) gets `404 not_found` in `StageRateLimit`, before the validator, which would answer a malformed path parameter with `400`; the key uses the canonical form of the ID. A panicking `Authenticator` settles the reserved `rl.public_auth` token like a server failure (returned), then the recover step answers `500`. For M1-34: everything except a counting `401` returns the token, so the identity ticket must report a failed expensive proof as a counting `401`, else the rule only bounds concurrent proofs. | The stage must not create a bucket for a value that names no webhook, and an unknown webhook is a 404 in the use case as well, so the answer leaks nothing; red team M1-19 round 2 found the 404 path, the panic path and the M1-34 dependency. |
| M1-19/13 | 2026-10-12 | Port onto main: `TestKeyKinds_MatchChapter05` maps the English words of the column "Key" of 05 "Rate-limit classes" (`client IP`, `device or token`, `principal`, `connection`, `agent`, `incoming webhook`, `host`) instead of the German ones; references name the English chapter files and headings of main; the planned rule `ratelimit.` leaves `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml`; the `jobs.go` row of the wire README names `MigrateUpSteps` (ruling M1-15/16). | Translation and conflict resolution only; the test still holds the hand-written map equal to the chapter table. |

### Test container cleanup (pgtest)

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| PGT-1/1 | 2026-10-13 | The `pgtest` container removes itself when its test binary is gone: the binary holds a lease connection, a watchdog script in the container stops PostgreSQL once the lease is missing for `leaseMisses` seconds, and `AutoRemove` removes the container with its volume. Once the lease was taken, `leaseStartGrace` without a readable answer stops a hung server as well, and the stop waits at most `leaseStopWait` before the script exits. A run label with an exit hook was not used. | No code in the process runs on `SIGKILL` or on the `-test.timeout` alarm, and Ryuk is off; only a check from outside the process covers every way a run ends, and the operating system closes the connection of a process however it ends. |
| PGT-1/2 | 2026-10-13 | `pgtest.Main` removes every container labelled `org.bothy.test` (any value) without the label `org.bothy.test.watchdog` once it is older than `legacyReapAge` (2 hours), with its volumes. This also covers the SeaweedFS helper of the files tests (`org.bothy.test=seaweedfs`), which is not on main yet; that helper may adopt a watchdog of its own when it lands. | One reaper for every labelled test container that cannot remove itself; a default `go test` run ends after 10 minutes (`-timeout`: "The default is 10 minutes (10m).", [go test flags](https://pkg.go.dev/cmd/go#hdr-Testing_flags), retrieved 2026-10-13), so a container that old belongs to a finished run. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |

| M1-28/D4 | 2026-10-12 | Visibility per resource kind as the reading of 07 section 4.3 for resources outside the structure tree: instance for every authenticated principal or a `Public` action; space with read on the space or on one of its channels; channel-path kinds (channel, thread, message, reaction, pin, run, run_step, component, interaction, agent_session, file, incoming_webhook, command) for dm participants only in a dm channel, otherwise with read on the path; grant for owners or with read on a space or channel target (instance and host targets: owner only); agent for owners, the agent itself or read on a channel it operates; user and principal for people and apps, or the principal itself; group for owners or members in effect; device, auth_session, api_token, passkey, push_registration, draft, gdpr_request and dev_environment for the owner of the resource, or an effective owner when the requirement names `owner`; app, webhook_delivery, import_source and host for owners. Any other kind (terminal_session included) is an internal error. Invisible is `not_found` with `no_grant`, `not_self` for the principal-owned kinds and `acting_for_insufficient` when only the acting-for intersection removed the visibility. On a space, `Level(read)` means "space visible". | 07 section 4.3 lists the rules for the tree and names the owned resources only by example; one table in `domain/visibility.go` makes every kind explicit and fails closed for a kind it does not know. The space rule was found by AZ-P8: a person who reads only a channel of a space must see the space (05 `space.read`). |

### CodeRabbit review tuning

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| CRT-1/1 | 2026-10-13 | Owner: CodeRabbit reviews with `profile: chill` instead of `assertive`, and a general path instruction for `**` asks it to report bugs, security issues, broken contracts and problems that break CI, without style, wording or formatting nits. The schema has no general review instruction outside `path_instructions` (`tone_instructions` sets the tone of reviews and chat, at most 250 characters), so the rule is a path instruction for every file. | Only Blockers and reproduced Majors block a merge; Minors are not polished. The assertive profile gives "more feedback (which may feel nitpicky)", chill "balanced feedback" ([schema v2](https://coderabbit.ai/integrations/schema.v2.json), retrieved 2026-10-13), and its nits cost review rounds without changing behavior. `tools/ci/tests/test_coderabbit_config.py` holds the profile and both instructions. |
| CRT-1/2 | 2026-10-13 | Owner: the path instruction for `**/*.md` keeps English only, no en or em dashes, no instance-specific or private data, glossary terms and named configuration values instead of bare limits; it asks for a source URL and retrieval date only for claims about external systems that the specification or the security documents rely on (`docs/spec/**`, `docs/security/**`, `docs/adr/**`). Every other setting of `.coderabbit.yaml` is unchanged. | Most CodeRabbit comments asked for a URL and retrieval date on every external fact in any Markdown file, which cost one to two hours per pull request; the sourcing rule matters where the specification and the security model build on the fact. |

### M3-01 Object storage port with file system driver

The brief names these rows D-M3-01-1 to D-M3-01-13; they follow the numbering of this log (M3-01/1 to M3-01/13), and M3-01/14 onwards are rulings of the build.

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M3-01/1 | 2026-10-12 | `ObjectStore` is an outgoing port of the application layer in `internal/files/app`, with its value types (`ByteRange`, `ObjectInfo`, `Part`, `PresignedPart`) and errors. The root package `internal/files` stays the incoming port for other modules, which never see `ObjectStore`. Key schema and key grammar are pure functions in `internal/files/domain`. The conformance suite is the test helper package `internal/files/adapters/filestest`, imported only by `_test.go` files; `TestFilestestNotInBinary` holds it out of `cmd/bothy`. | 02 "5. Layers within a module": the application layer holds outgoing ports as interfaces, adapters implement them; 02 "6. Code layout" places test helpers in `adapters/<module>test`. |
| M3-01/2 | 2026-10-12 | Error contract of the port: `ErrInvalidKey` (the same value as `domain.ErrInvalidKey`, checked before any storage call), `ErrNotFound`, `ErrInvalidRange`, `ErrSizeMismatch`, `ErrUploadNotFound`, `ErrInvalidPart` (the upload stays open, the list shape is checked before any service call), `ErrNotSupported`. `Delete` of a missing object and `AbortMultipart` of an unknown, aborted or completed upload return nil. Every other error is a storage failure (`503 unavailable`); errors wrap the cause, name operation and key, never contain object bytes, and a refused key is not repeated. Recorded in 12 "4.1". | 12 "4.1" and "12": a missing object counts as deleted, so retried cleanup jobs converge; callers decide `503` against a client error by sentinel only, and a key a caller passed may carry anything. |
| M3-01/3 | 2026-10-12 | `Get` takes at most one byte range `{Offset, Length}`; a range whose end lies beyond the object ends at the last byte, `Offset < 0`, `Length < 1` and `Offset` at or beyond the size give `ErrInvalidRange`. | 12 "9.3" supports exactly one range, and an HTTP range past the end is cut there; an offset beyond the end has no byte to return (`416` for M3-05). |
| M3-01/4 | 2026-10-12 | `ValidateKey` is one grammar for all drivers: segments of `[a-z0-9-]` joined by single `/`, no leading or trailing `/`. `filepath.Clean(key) == key` holds for every accepted key; `FuzzValidateKey` holds the grammar. | ADR 0010: the key schema is the same in both drivers, so a key that works on one works on the other, and no key reaches outside the root. |
| M3-01/5 | 2026-10-12 | On the file system `CreateMultipart` accepts only original keys, creates `uploads/<file_id>/`, is idempotent and returns the upload ID `""`; `UploadPart` and `CompleteMultipart` need the ID `""` and that directory, else `ErrUploadNotFound`. While an upload is open, its parts are regular objects under `uploads/<file_id>/<n>`; the conformance suite never relies on that. The driver takes no lock. | 03 `file_upload_sessions.storage_upload_id` holds an upload ID only for the S3 driver, and the place of the parts follows from the key (12 "3.2"); clients upload only originals in parts; the session row of M3-03 serializes completion against late parts. |
| M3-01/6 | 2026-10-12 | The file system part ETag is the lower-case hex SHA-256 of the part bytes in double quotes, computed while writing; `CompleteMultipart` reads each listed part exactly once into one temporary file, checks its hash against the ETag (`ErrInvalidPart` on a mismatch, the upload stays), and removes `uploads/<file_id>/` with all parts, listed or not; a failure of that removal after the rename does not fail the completion. | `UploadPartResult.etag` is required for both drivers, and the form equals the `ETag` header of 12 "9.3"; one read per part follows R-FI-04; S3 also discards unlisted parts; a retry after a reported cleanup failure would find parts missing and refuse a finished object, while the `Delete` of the original removes leftover parts (M3-01/18). |
| M3-01/7 | 2026-10-12 | Temporary files of writes lie in `<root>/.tmp` with mode `0600` and a random name; every failed write removes its own. | `.tmp` starts with a dot, which no key may contain, so no key addresses a temporary file; the same file system makes the rename atomic. |
| M3-01/8 | 2026-10-12 | The store keeps an `os.Root` of the root and in addition follows no symbolic link at all: each existing path component is checked with `Lstat` through the root before an operation and again before the rename. `os.Root` follows a link inside the root even with `O_NOFOLLOW` (Go `rootOpenFileNolog` resolves `ELOOP` itself, except with `O_CREATE\|O_EXCL`), so a read compares the opened file with the checked one (`os.SameFile`) and on a difference checks the path again: a link there is a storage failure, another regular file is a concurrent replace and a valid read (`TestGetRefusesLinkSwappedBeforeOpen`). Unix-only file; on other systems `Open` refuses every root. A link found is a storage failure, not `ErrNotFound`. | 12 "4.2": the driver follows no symbolic link; `os.Root` alone keeps operations inside the root but would follow links within it; a storage failure makes the cause visible to the operator. Residual window: a process that can write into the root could swap a component for a link between the check and the call; `os.Root` still keeps that inside the root, and only the server user can write there (start check). Such a process could also put a link in and replace it by a regular file again between the open and the second check; the read then returns the bytes of the link target, still an object inside the root. |
| M3-01/9 | 2026-10-12 | `fs.Open` checks the root: absolute and clean, exists, `Lstat` shows a directory and not a link, owned by the effective user, permission bits exactly `0700` (no setuid, setgid or sticky bit), the opened handle is the same directory as the checked one. It creates `<root>/.tmp` with mode `0700` when missing, refuses a `.tmp` that is not a directory of the same user with mode exactly `0700`, never creates the root and never removes entries of `.tmp`. Errors name `file.fs.root` and the expected state, never the path. | The same start check as a server-owned key directory (M1-32); a missing root means a wrong mount, and entries of `.tmp` may belong to another process on the same root. |
| M3-01/10 | 2026-10-12 | `Put` on an existing key replaces it atomically; the drivers do not enforce "Objects are never overwritten". | The module enforces it through the key schema (a changed original gets the next revision) and the unique `files.object_key`; a retried job may rewrite a key that no committed row names yet. |
| M3-01/11 | 2026-10-12 | `Delete` removes the file, syncs the directory, then removes `f/<aa>/<bb>/<file_id>` or `uploads/<file_id>` if it is empty now ("not empty" and "already gone" are ignored); the fan-out levels stay. A writer whose directory vanished before the rename creates it again and renames again (see M3-01/15). Deleting the last part of an open upload removes `uploads/<file_id>/` and so ends the upload. | Without it every deleted file would leave a directory behind, and the server never lists to find them (12 "4.1"). |
| M3-01/12 | 2026-10-12 | Cross-check `file_fs_root_absolute` on `file.storage.driver` and `file.fs.root`: with `fs` the root must satisfy `filepath.IsAbs` and `filepath.Clean(root) == root`; with `s3` nothing is checked. Recorded in 02 "13. Configuration loading" item 4. | A relative root would depend on the working directory of the process; with `s3` the root is unused. |
| M3-01/13 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.FilesObjectStore` refuses the driver `s3` with `config: file.storage.driver: driver s3 is not supported by this server version` until M3-02 replaces the branch. No role calls `FilesObjectStore` yet. | As M1-07 did for the secret schemes `azkv` and `awssm`; M3-03 opens the store in the roles together with the image directory, because opening it at start now would stop the stack. |
| M3-01/14 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M3-01-object-storage-fs` from `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. Text added to the German chapters 12 and 02 and to ADR 0010 is English, as is the README addition of `files`. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's merge step and over "German like the chapter"; everything added is English (M1-23/9, M1-19/11). |
| M3-01/15 | 2026-10-12 | The rename of a write repeats (directory created again) as long as it fails because a concurrent `Delete` removed the emptied file directory, instead of the single retry of the brief; it stops when the rename succeeds, fails for another reason, the temporary file is gone or the context ends. "Vanished" is `ENOENT`, `EINVAL` from the rename, or a directory that is missing now; `mkdirs` creates a component again that vanished between `Mkdir` and `Lstat`. A directory sync after the rename that finds the directory gone is no error. | `TestTwoStoresShareRoot` (two stores, parallel `Put`, `Get` and `Delete` on keys of the same files) failed with a single retry on macOS: renameat returns `EINVAL`, not `ENOENT`, when a concurrent rmdir removes the target directory during the call (reproduced in a probe), and two removals can fall into one write. Each repetition needs another delete that empties the directory in that window, so no count is needed; a count would be a threshold without a configuration key. |
| M3-01/16 | 2026-10-12 | `*fs.Store` has `Close` (releases the root handle) besides the port; `FilesObjectStore` returns the store as `filesapp.ObjectStore`, which implements `io.Closer` for the driver `fs`, and never a typed nil. | The `os.Root` holds a file descriptor; the port has no lifecycle operation (12 "4.1"), so the owner of the store (M3-03) closes it through `io.Closer`. |
| M3-01/17 | 2026-10-12 | Every directory a write creates is synced in its parent (`TestNewDirectoriesAreSynced`), beyond the sync of the target directory that the brief names. `CompleteMultipart` checks the shape of the part list before any file system call, also before the upload check, and the conformance suite holds it (an empty list on an unknown upload is `ErrInvalidPart`). | Code review M3-01 (findings 2 and 3): without the parent sync a crash after a successful `Put` can lose the entry of a new file directory on file systems without ordered metadata; the list check first keeps both drivers equal (design note 3). A writer that finds a directory already created by a concurrent writer does not sync its parent again; the creator does, and an extra fsync per existing level on every write would cost more than the window between another writer's `Mkdir` and its parent sync. |
| M3-01/18 | 2026-10-12 | `Delete` of an original key also removes `uploads/<file_id>/` with any parts in it (`TestCompleteSurvivesCleanupFailure`), also when the object itself is already gone. | Code re-review M3-01: when `CompleteMultipart` cannot remove the parts after the rename, nothing else would ever remove them (a completed session never expires, the server never lists), and deleting the file must remove all its bytes (12 "12", also for GDPR erasure). An upload in parts exists only for a file that has no committed original yet, and the delete of a failed upload wants its parts gone too. |
| M3-01/19 | 2026-10-12 | Red team M3-01 (verdict JA, five minors): `PresignUploadPart` checks the part number (`ErrInvalidPart` for `n < 1`) before it answers `ErrNotSupported`, as the port contract says for every driver; `TestKeyBuildersTakeOnlyIDsAndNumbers` finds every exported `...Key` function of `internal/files/domain` in the source, so a new builder cannot escape the parameter check. Not changed, by decision: an upload directory left by a failed cleanup after `CompleteMultipart` still accepts parts until the original is deleted (the session row of M3-03 refuses parts and completion after `completed`); a file system upload is bound to the file ID, not to the revision of the key (only `orig-1` is uploaded in parts, 12 "3.1"); `Put` on a part key creates `uploads/<file_id>` (parts are objects, M3-01/5, and no caller writes part keys with `Put`). | Design note 3 puts the part number rule into the port for both drivers, and design note 11 only forbids file system calls; the other three cases need a caller that M3-01 does not have, and guarding them in the driver would duplicate the session state of M3-03. |
| M3-01/20 | 2026-10-14 | `sonar-project.properties` leaves the test helper packages `services/server/internal/*/adapters/*test/` (here `filestest`) out of the coverage measure (`sonar.coverage.exclusions`); they stay in the analysis for issues. | Go counts the coverage of a package only from its own tests, and a test helper is exercised by the tests of the drivers that import it, so its failure branches (`t.Error` calls) would read as uncovered new code and fail the gate on coverage of new code. |

### M3-02 S3 driver against SeaweedFS

The brief names these rows D-M3-02-1 to D-M3-02-13; they follow the numbering of this log (M3-02/1 to M3-02/13), and M3-02/14 onwards are rulings of the build.

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M3-02/1 | 2026-10-12 | One egress client per store: purpose `objectstore`, a host policy that allows exactly the scheme, host and port of every request (the endpoint with path style, `<bucket>.<endpoint host>` with virtual-hosted style, the port of the endpoint or the default port of its scheme), `RedirectNever`, the time limit `http.transfer_timeout` and the body limit `file.max_upload_bytes`. | 19 SEC-NET-2: a consumer names its own targets. 02 14.2 lets a chapter name the limit of an outside call, and these bytes serve uploads and downloads, whose limit `http.transfer_timeout` is. No stored object is larger than `file.max_upload_bytes` (03 `files.size_bytes`), so a body is neither cut nor unbounded (SEC-NET-5). An S3 redirect means a wrong endpoint or region. |
| M3-02/2 | 2026-10-12 | The SDK client is built by hand with `s3.New(s3.Options{...})`: region, the credentials of M3-02/6, the HTTP client of the egress client, the checksum mode of M3-02/10, a no-op logger, no tracer or meter, `BaseEndpoint` from `file.s3.endpoint`, `UsePathStyle`, S3 Express session auth off. `github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2/config` and `LoadDefaultConfig` are never used, and `file.s3.endpoint` is required. The brief named `aws.Config` plus `s3.NewFromConfig`; `NewFromConfig` looks up `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL` and related variables of the environment, `s3.New` takes only the options. | The default configuration reads the environment and shared files and asks the instance metadata service, whose address SEC-NET-3 always blocks and whose credentials 12 4.3 does not allow. Without the endpoint resolution of the SDK the host policy names exactly one host. SDK spans and metrics have no names in `signals.md` (02 5 rule 5). The SDK still reads `AWS_EXECUTION_ENV` and the Lambda trace variables for the user agent and the recursion header; neither changes a target or a credential. |
| M3-02/3 | 2026-10-12 | No SDK retries: `aws.NopRetryer{}`. A failed call is one request and a storage failure. | 02 14.3: no retry of external calls in the request path; jobs retry through River. `TestNoRetries` holds one request for a `503 SlowDown`. |
| M3-02/4 | 2026-10-12 | `Put` and `UploadPart` sign their body as `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` (`v4.SwapComputePayloadSHA256ForUnsignedPayloadMiddleware`, present in `aws-sdk-go-v2` v1.47.3) and stream it with `ContentLength` set, never buffered. | The caller's reader is not seekable and the endpoint may be plain `http` in a private network, where the SDK cannot hash the payload before sending. Integrity comes from the part digest (12 3.2) and the file SHA-256 of the read pass (12 3.4), which 12 4.3 names as the replacement of SDK checksums. |
| M3-02/5 | 2026-10-12 | Every error of the driver is one type with the text `files/s3: <operation> <key>: <detail>`; detail is the port sentinel, the S3 code with the HTTP status, a status, a fixed text for a known cause (egress refusal with its reason, response too large, context cancelled or deadline, credentials with the key and the secret error code) or `transport`. `Unwrap` returns the port sentinel and the cause. Mapping: `NoSuchKey`, `NotFound` or 404 on `Get` and `Head` is `ErrNotFound` (`NoSuchBucket` is a storage failure); `InvalidRange` or 416 on `Get` is `ErrInvalidRange`; `NoSuchUpload` on `UploadPart` and `CompleteMultipart` is `ErrUploadNotFound` and on `AbortMultipart` nil; `InvalidPart`, `InvalidPartOrder` and `EntityTooSmall` on `CompleteMultipart` are `ErrInvalidPart`; everything else is a storage failure. The brief wrote the prefix as `s3 <operation>`; the driver uses `files/s3:` like the file system driver's `files/fs:`. | net/http errors carry the full request URL, and callers that wrap a port error into `kernel/errs` put its text into the log (SEC-LOG-3, SEC-SEC-3). The conformance suite gives the same results on both drivers. A HEAD answer has no body, so `Head` on a missing bucket reads as `ErrNotFound`; `Get`, `Put` and `Delete` report the bucket as a storage failure. |
| M3-02/6 | 2026-10-12 | The credentials provider resolves both references through the secret provider on every `Retrieve` and returns credentials that expire at once, so the SDK credential cache asks again for the next request; the driver keeps no copy. `New` resolves both once and stops with an error that names the key. `bothy config check --secrets` resolves the two references only with `file.storage.driver = s3` and prints `skipped` otherwise. | 16 5.2 and 02 13 item 6: a rotation works without restart once the provider re-reads the file after `secrets.cache_ttl`; a failed re-read keeps the old value inside the provider (M1-09). 02 14.4 row Secret-Provider: fail fast at start. 12 18: the references are read only with the driver `s3`. |
| M3-02/7 | 2026-10-12 | The object name is `<file.s3.prefix>/<key>` with a prefix, else the key; key and prefix both pass `domain.ValidateKey` before any request, and a refused key or prefix is never repeated in the error. | M3-01/4: one grammar for all drivers, which refuses empty segments and a leading or trailing `/`, so a path-style URL never collapses two keys into one object (`FuzzObjectName`); the driver adds nothing else to a key (SEC-UP-8). |
| M3-02/8 | 2026-10-12 | A body of another length than `size` gives `ErrSizeMismatch` and stores nothing: the body wrapper hands out at most `size - 1` bytes until the source has delivered the last byte and reported its end. A longer source fails before the last byte leaves, a shorter one fails before `ContentLength` is reached; a size of 0 proves the reader empty before any request. | M3-01 note 3. S3 never receives a complete request, so neither `PutObject` nor `UploadPart` can store a body of the wrong length (`casePutSizeMismatch`, `TestS3UploadPartSizeMismatch` against SeaweedFS). |
| M3-02/9 | 2026-10-12 | The port operation is `PresignUploadPart(ctx, key, uploadID, n, size, ttl)`. The S3 driver presigns `UploadPart` with `ContentLength = size` and the expiry `ttl`, and returns `ExpiresAt` = the time taken before signing plus `ttl`. `n < 1` (or above 10000) gives `ErrInvalidPart`, `size < 1` `ErrSizeMismatch` on both drivers; a `ttl` that is not a positive whole number of seconds and an empty upload ID are refused before signing. 12 4.1 is updated (chapter 12 is not core contract). Result of `TestPresignedPartBindsLength` (12 3.3): SeaweedFS at the pinned version accepts a plain `PUT` of exactly the signed length and refuses one byte more, one byte less, chunked transfer encoding and a `PUT` after the validity, each without a part. | 12 3.3, R-FI-02, RK-42: no driver can bind a length it does not know, and `X-Amz-Expires` carries whole seconds. The mode `presigned` stays off (`file.s3.presigned_upload` is not registered); client reachability of the storage stays open. |
| M3-02/10 | 2026-10-12 | `file.s3.checksum_mode` has the values `when_required` (default) and `when_supported`; they map to `aws.RequestChecksumCalculationWhenRequired` with `aws.ResponseChecksumValidationWhenRequired` and to the two `WhenSupported` values. `when_supported` is refused at load with an `http` endpoint (cross-check `file_s3`). | 12 18 names the default but not the value set; the two values are the two settings of the SDK, the same for request and response as 12 4.3 says. With `when_supported` the SDK sends the checksum of a streamed, unseekable body as a trailer and refuses that without TLS, so every `Put` and `UploadPart` over `http` would fail (found in review; `TestChecksumWhenSupportedOverHTTPS` holds the https path). |
| M3-02/11 | 2026-10-12 | Cross-check `file_s3` over `file.storage.driver`, the six non-secret `file.s3.*` keys and `egress.allowed_hosts`, only with driver `s3`, through `s3.Config.Validate`: endpoint set and an absolute `http` or `https` URL with a host and without user info, query, fragment or a path other than `/`; bucket set and in the S3 naming rules for general purpose buckets (reserved prefixes and suffixes such as `--x-s3` refused); region set; prefix empty or the key grammar; an IP endpoint host only with path style; an `http` endpoint only with an `egress.allowed_hosts` entry with `allow_private` for the request host and port. `file.s3.endpoint` is a string key. The check fills `s3.Config` from the six keys; it cannot bind the struct, because a check sees only the keys it names. `New` runs the same `Validate`. | 02 13 item 4, every message names keys, never values. 19 SEC-NET-2 allows `http` only with such an entry; without it the server would start and fail at the first request. A directory bucket would make the SDK open a session with `CreateSession`, which is not core S3. The schema allows an empty default only for string and list keys. |
| M3-02/12 | 2026-10-12 | Architecture rule: vendor group `awssdk` (`github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2`, `github.com/aws/smithy-go` and their subpackages) and `adapter_vendors: [awssdk]` for module `files`; depguard rule `aws-sdk-only-in-s3-driver` denies `github.com/aws/` in every file except `**/internal/files/adapters/s3/**`, production and test files alike; forbidigo forbids `s3.Client.DeleteObjects` and `s3.PresignClient.PresignGetObject`. A temporary SDK import in `adapters/fs` and temporary calls in the s3 package failed `golangci-lint` once and were reverted. | 12 15, ADR 0010 and ADR 0048 Confirmation. go-arch-lint grants vendors to the component `internal/files/adapters/**` as a whole, so only depguard (ADR 0008, rules per file pattern) narrows the SDK to one adapter. |
| M3-02/13 | 2026-10-12 | The SeaweedFS test container runs `chrislusf/seaweedfs:4.48` pinned by the digest of its multi-architecture index, the repository and version of the Compose service `seaweedfs` (`TestSeaweedImagePinned`; Renovate moves it in the group Compose-Images through its own regex manager). It starts with the command of the Compose service (`weed server -dir=/data -s3 -ip.bind=0.0.0.0`, keys as `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`) plus `-master.volumeSizeLimitMB=32 -volume.max=1000`. Test code builds a full SDK client to create buckets and inspect state (`ListParts`, `HeadObject`); the driver never does. `infra/compose/compose.yaml` stays unchanged. | The test proves the version the stack runs. SeaweedFS keeps every bucket in its own volumes, and with the default volume size of 30 GB the free disk of a test runtime held one bucket only (`No writable volumes and no free volumes left`), while every test creates its own bucket; the stack has one bucket. `TestS3WrongCredentialsRefused` shows that SeaweedFS with this command refuses a wrong secret key, so the Compose service needs no identity configuration. |
| M3-02/14 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.FilesObjectStore(ctx, values, wire.FilesDeps{Secrets, Egress})`: driver `fs` unchanged; driver `s3` binds `s3.Config`, builds `wire.EgressConfig` and calls `s3.New` with `http.transfer_timeout` and `file.max_upload_bytes`. A construction error is an error of `FilesObjectStore` that starts with `config:` and names the key. No role opens the store yet. | M3-01/13: M3-03 opens the store in the roles together with the image directory. The S3 branch needs a context, the secret provider and the egress dependencies, which the old signature did not carry; no other egress consumer is wired yet, so `FilesDeps.Egress` is where the logger and metrics of the process go. |
| M3-02/15 | 2026-10-12 | The ticket ends on the branch `feat/M3-02-object-storage-s3` from `int/m1-wave6` and is not merged locally; the worktree stays. | The launcher of this build overrides the brief's "merge locally into the base branch, then `git worktree remove`": the integration of the wave merges the ticket branches. |

Each direction keeps its own counter and refuses at 2^64 - 1 (`ErrNonceExhausted`); a call out of order gives `ErrState`. The limits are checked by the wrapper before the library is called. Every failure of a Dart handshake step breaks the object, whatever its type. Recorded in 24 5.2. | Both sides must behave the same, and an all-zero shared secret means a key that an attacker chose; the wrapper keeps the rule independent of the library version. |

Every public `List<int>` input of `bothy_noise` rejects a value outside 0 to 255 (`lib/src/bytes.dart`): a received message or frame fails like any other bad input of its step (`handshakeFailed`, `decryptFailed`, `malformed`) and breaks the object, a bad key or ID throws `invalidKey` or `invalidId`, and a bad local payload, plaintext or prologue throws an `ArgumentError` and changes no state; and an overlapping `decrypt` that resolves after a failed one throws `broken`. | Red team M2-38 (findings 2, 4, 5): `flutter analyze` and `flutter test` rewrote a stale lock file silently, so `make osv` and `make licenses` alone could check another lock than the tests ran with; `Uint8List.fromList` truncated such values silently where Go cannot receive them; the broken rule must hold for callers that do not await each call. |
| D-M2-38-22 | 2026-10-14 | The root Makefile runs Flutter as `$(FLUTTER)`, `flutter` without the variables `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_PREFIX`, in `make lint` and `make test`; `test_flutter_runs_without_the_git_variables_of_a_hook` holds it. | git exports these variables to hooks, and the pre-push hook runs `make check`; Flutter reads its own version with git inside the SDK, so under a hook it read this repository, called its version unknown and refused the pinned SDK version of `pubspec.yaml`. The same rule already covers `make api-clients`. |

### M1-16 Event log with commit-ordered sequence

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-16/1 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M1-16-event-log` in its own worktree from the head of `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done"). |
| M1-16/2 | 2026-10-12 | A payload must be smaller than `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` (a payload of exactly the limit is rejected), as 04 "Rules for the payload" item 2 ("smaller than") and the fixed requirement 16 of the brief ("below") say; 03 ("up to") and the design note ("at most") read as an upper bound the strict rule also keeps. | The core contract chapters disagree by one byte; the strict reading satisfies every text. |
| M1-16/3 | 2026-10-12 | The appender also rejects a payload whose strings or keys contain U+0000. | jsonb cannot store U+0000 (SQLSTATE 22P05, `untranslatable_character`; [JSON types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-json.html) and [PostgreSQL error codes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/errcodes-appendix.html), retrieved 2026-10-13); the producer would otherwise get a database error inside the hook instead of a kind internal error at the append. |
| M1-16/4 | 2026-10-12 | `Event.Envelope` and the mapping rules live in the port `internal/sync`; `domain/` holds the validation, the partition arithmetic and the gap check. | The brief asks for the method on the port type and for the rules in domain; the port may import only kernel packages (`.go-arch-lint.yml`), so it cannot call domain. |
| M1-16/5 | 2026-10-12 | `DeliveryFields` carries `SyncEpoch` as well (`sync_epoch`, webhooks only, ADR 0059). | `EventEnvelope` in openapi.yaml has the member, and `TestEnvelope_FieldsMatchOpenAPI` requires the envelope members to equal its properties. |
| M1-16/6 | 2026-10-12 | The dedicated connection is `(*pg.Pool).DialDedicated(ctx, applicationName)` instead of a free function `pg.DialDedicated`. | A connection with the configuration and password source of the pool needs both; the pool already holds them. |
| M1-16/7 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.MigrateUpSteps(pool, fsys, MigrateUpConfig{JobsSchema, Sync}, result)` adds the after-step `event_log_partitions` behind the instance step; `wire.MigrationSteps`, which `pgtest.WithSteps` uses for templates, keeps only the instance step, so test databases have no partitions and the event log tests create theirs with their own size. | `bothy migrate up` must use the configured values (brief scope item 9); pgtest's step function gets no configuration. |
| M1-16/8 | 2026-10-12 | The new partition table is `LIKE event_log INCLUDING DEFAULTS INCLUDING CONSTRAINTS`. | ATTACH PARTITION requires the CHECK constraints of the parent on the attached table ([ALTER TABLE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-altertable.html), retrieved 2026-10-13); with `INCLUDING DEFAULTS` alone it fails. |
| M1-16/9 | 2026-10-12 | `sync_event_log_ensure_partitions` locks `event_log` in `SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE` mode before it reads the bounds; `sync_event_log_drop_expired` stops at the first partition it keeps, so only a prefix of partitions is ever dropped. | Two concurrent runs (job and `bothy migrate up`) would otherwise create the same table twice; `occurred_at` is the start of a transaction and need not grow with `seq` (`now()` returns the transaction start, [Date/Time functions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/functions-datetime.html), retrieved 2026-10-13), and a hole in the retained range would break `min_retained_seq` (04 "resync_required"). |
| M1-16/10 | 2026-10-12 | The rows go in with one multi-row insert from arrays, and `pg_notify` runs in the same statement (a data-modifying CTE), not with sqlc `:copyfrom` and not as a second statement. | Every round trip under the counter lock lowers the commit rate (`perf.event_commit_min_per_s`); one statement is one round trip, COPY would need a second one for the notification. |
| M1-16/11 | 2026-10-12 | Without a valid span context the correlation ID of the request in `kernel/reqmeta` (if it is a UUID) becomes `correlation_id`; `trace_context` stays empty. Without a span and outside a request both are NULL. | 03 `event_log.correlation_id`: a request without traceparent gets an ID from the server; `platform/httpserver` keeps that ID in reqmeta, the same the response and the audit entry carry. |
| M1-16/12 | 2026-10-12 | The error line of an insert without a partition carries `bothy.event.seq` (the first seq) and `error.type` (`23514`); the whole range is in the error text. | The log handlers keep only catalogue attributes (`platform/otel/attr`), and the catalogue has no second seq attribute; adding one is a telemetry change outside this ticket. |
| M1-16/13 | 2026-10-12 | Bounds of the new keys: `sync.event_log_partition_size` at least 1, `sync.event_retention` at least 1s, `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` 3 to 1048576, `sync.log_poll_interval` at least 1ms, `sync.tail_batch_size` 1 to 2147483647 (the PostgreSQL `integer` maximum, [Numeric types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-numeric.html), retrieved 2026-10-13), `sync.partition_maintenance_interval` at least 1s, `sync.event_log_partitions_ahead` 1 to 1000. | The chapters give only defaults; `{}` is the smallest payload, 0 partitions ahead would let the next commit miss a partition, and a payload limit above `http.max_json_body_bytes` would never be reached by a request. |
| M1-16/14 | 2026-10-12 | The tail's start step waits until `Run` has read the head (`Tail.Started`) before the public listener opens; a tail that ends with an error calls `Runner.Fail`, and its stop step `event_tail` runs in `PhaseEventTail` without its own budget. | 02 "Start and graceful shutdown" step 8 sets `local_head` before the listener; the stop only cancels a loop. |
| M1-16/15 | 2026-10-12 | ADR 0009 is not changed; its confirmation is held by `TestEventLog_ConcurrentWritersGapless` and `TestTail_ListenConnectionLossLosesNothing`. | The brief extends only ADR 0052, 0053 and 0063. |
| M1-16/16 | 2026-10-12 | `make perf` measured `perf.event_commit_min_per_s` below the threshold on a development machine whose database runs in a VM (one round trip about 0.4ms from the test process); the gate stays as ADR 0063 defines it and the numbers are in the report, not in docs. | Under the counter lock every unit spends about three round trips and the commit, so the rate there is bound by the VM network, not by the pattern; the reference profile runs PostgreSQL locally (02 "Configuration values of this chapter", `perf.reference_profile`). |
| M1-16/17 | 2026-10-12 | The module README of sync keeps its generated sections (purpose, port, dependencies, tables, actions, events, jobs), which `make register-check` compares with the register, and adds the sections of this ticket. | The generated sections are not this ticket's text. |
| M1-16/18 | 2026-10-12 | The appender rejects numbers with an exponent, invalid UTF-8 and unpaired surrogate escapes, and the reads compact the jsonb text before they hand payloads to the sinks. | Red team round 1 (M1): jsonb stores numbers as `numeric` and writes `1e300` out in full ([JSON types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-json.html), retrieved 2026-10-13), so a checked payload could be stored and delivered far above `sync.event_payload_max_bytes`; without exponents the compact form of the normalised jsonb is never larger than the checked bytes. |
| M1-16/19 | 2026-10-12 | The partition maintenance runs `ensure` and `drop_expired` in two units of work; an error of the drop is `ErrDropDeferred` with the result of the first unit, the job is retried by River, and the partition step of `bothy migrate up` ignores it. | Red team round 1 (M2): a DETACH waiting behind a long reader rolled back the partitions created in the same unit, and appends ran out of partitions. |
| M1-16/20 | 2026-10-12 | `sync_event_log_drop_expired` clamps `p_head` to `max(seq)` and `p_cutoff` to `now()`; `sync_event_log_ensure_partitions` refuses a size above 10^12 and more than 1000 partitions ahead, and `sync.event_log_partition_size` has the maximum 10^12. | Red team round 1 (Minor, hardening): bothy_app could drop the head partition with arguments of its choice, and an unbounded size overflows bigint in `p_head + p_ahead * p_size`. |
| M1-16/21 | 2026-10-12 | A sink error while the context of `Tail.Run` has ended is the orderly end of `Run` (nil), not a fatal error; a stored payload `null` renders as `{}` in the envelope. | Red team round 1 (Minor): a sink that honours the context would otherwise turn every shutdown into exit code 1. |
| M1-16/22 | 2026-10-12 | Numbers with more than 131072 digits before or 16383 digits after the point (the `numeric` limits, [Numeric types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-numeric.html), retrieved 2026-10-13) are rejected at the append; only a lock timeout of the drop unit becomes `ErrDropDeferred`, any other drop error is returned unchanged. | Red team round 2 (M1-rest): such numbers passed the appender and failed the unit at the insert with SQLSTATE 22003 (`numeric_value_out_of_range`); a permanent drop failure must not pass silently as deferred. |
| M1-16/23 | 2026-10-12 | Long digit runs (17 to 20 digits) never appear as literals in tests: names and limits are built (`fmt.Sprintf("%019d", ...)`, `math.MaxInt64` as a parameter), and the generic white-label scan (`tools/denylist`, `make test` and `make denylist`) runs on the working tree before every commit of this ticket. | Red team rounds 1 and 2 (B1): the generic rule for account and card numbers matched partition names and the bigint maximum twice; the gate that catches the class exists, the gap was running it before the commit. |
| M1-16/24 | 2026-10-12 | Port onto main, after M1-28: the start step of the event log is 6c (`sync`), after 6b (`authz`, M1-28); both come from the same base, and neither depends on the other. The load test report (`perf/internal/report`) reads the English section "Configuration values of this chapter" of 02 and the place `Load test`; the build notes go into the English ADRs 0052, 0053 and 0063; references name the English chapter files and headings of main. | Translation and conflict resolution only; the behaviour equals the approved ticket. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| PL/1 | 2026-10-14 | `make test` runs the Go tests with `-coverpkg=./...`: a statement counts as covered when any test of its module runs it, also a test in another package. The gate `coverage` (`quality.coverage.go_min_percent`) and the new-code coverage of SonarQube read this profile. | Many use cases are tested through their HTTP adapter against PostgreSQL (the coming sessions, refresh and step-up flows among them); with per-package coverage the code those tests run counted as uncovered, and the new-code gate failed for code that the suite executes. |

## M1-20 Idempotency keys

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-20/1 | 2026-10-12 | Fingerprint (`request_sha256`): SHA-256 over the upper-case method, a line feed, `r.URL.EscapedPath()` (with `/api/v1`, without query), a line feed and the canonical body: the RFC 8785 form when the body is one JSON value without a duplicate member (`platform/jsoncanon` over `github.com/gowebpki/jcs` v1.0.2, Apache-2.0), the raw bytes otherwise, empty without a body. Recorded in 06 "Idempotency" point 6. | 04 and 03 name "method, path and canonical body"; RFC 8785 makes member order, whitespace and escapes irrelevant, so a client may re-serialise its outbox entry. The raw bytes keep a malformed body comparable, and StageBody answers it with a final `400` that is stored under the same fingerprint. |
| M1-20/2 | 2026-10-12 | The header has no effect on an operation without the parameter `IdempotencyKey` and for the anonymous actor; the request runs as without it. Where it acts, a value that is not one UUIDv7 is `400 validation_failed` (`#header/Idempotency-Key`, `invalid_format`) and nothing is stored. Recorded in 06 "Idempotency" point 6. | `idempotency_keys.principal_id` references `principals`, and no public operation declares the parameter; storing per anonymous client would key on nothing stable. |
| M1-20/3 | 2026-10-12 | `jsoncanon.Canonicalize` refuses an integer literal (no fraction, no exponent) that a double cannot hold exactly; the fingerprint then uses the raw bytes. Literals with a fraction or an exponent are rounded as RFC 8785 says. | RFC 8785 rounds every number to a double, so 2^64 - 1 and 2^64 - 2 written out as integers would share one fingerprint and the second request would get the first one's response instead of `422`. The API carries no such integer (ADR 0053), so the rule costs nothing for valid requests. |
| M1-20/4 | 2026-10-12 | The stage runs the rest of the chain in the outermost unit of work without `txn.RightsChange`. A keyed use case that needs `RightsChange` (M1-48 `createGrant`, `revokeGrant`, `addGroupMember`; M1-52 to M1-54) gets the internal error of `platform/pg` for a nested `RightsChange` until the ticket that builds the first such handler resolves it. Recommended there: the route metadata marks rights-changing operations and the stage opens its unit with `RightsChange`, or `platform/pg` lets a nested `RightsChange` run the rights begin hook when the unit has written nothing but the key row (the key row is the first and only lock of `idempotency_keys` in every unit, so taking `permission_version` after it cannot close a cycle). | 02 "Idempotency in the Unit of Work" makes the stage open the unit, and 02 "Flow" lets only the outermost unit declare `RightsChange`; the affected principals are known only inside the use case. Changing `kernel/txn` or `platform/pg` (M1-13) is outside the scope of this ticket, and no handler with `RightsChange` exists yet (`api/operations.yaml` enables no operation). |
| M1-20/5 | 2026-10-12 | A deadlock or lock timeout (SQLSTATE `40P01` or `55P03`, read through `SQLState()`) that reaches `WriteProblem` inside an attempt of the stage ends the attempt with that error, so the unit of work of the stage retries it. | Inside the stage the use case's unit of work joins the stage's one and returns the database error to its handler, which writes a problem; without this the retry of 02 "Lock order" would be lost for every keyed operation. Each attempt reads the body again and starts with an empty buffer (`TestRetry_ReinvokesWithFreshBuffer`, `TestRetry_HandlerDeadlockReachesTheUnitOfWork`). |
| M1-20/6 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed operation with a file body (multipart or octet-stream) answers `500 internal_error`; a keyed `2xx` whose body is not JSON rolls back and answers `500`; a final error without a JSON body is sent but not stored. | The generator refuses all three forms for the contract (`api/routes/gen`), so they can only come from a hand-built operation or a handler bug; failing closed never hashes an upload in memory and never stores a body that `jsonb` cannot hold. |
| M1-20/7 | 2026-10-12 | A stored success that carried a one-time secret is recognised by its body `{"one_time_secret": true}` with a `2xx` status; its replay is `409 conflict`, `reason: one_time_secret`, `hint: create a new one`, `Idempotent-Replay: true`. | The brief fixes the placeholder; the table has no column for the mark, and a contract response never consists of exactly this member. |
| M1-20/8 | 2026-10-12 | Generator checks for every keyed operation of `openapi.yaml`, enabled or not: no query parameter, request body absent or `application/json`, every `2xx` with `application/json` or no content, and a `2xx` with `ETag` has a required integer member `version` (also through `allOf`). | Brief "Generator checks"; checking all operations now keeps a later handler ticket from enabling an operation whose replay cannot be rebuilt (`TestRealSpec_KeyedOperationsReplayable`). |
| M1-20/9 | 2026-10-12 | Built on `feat/M1-20-idempotency-keys` from `int/m1-wave6` (`785916c`) in its own worktree; not merged locally, not pushed; the PR lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00007`. | The launcher's task overrides the brief's "merge locally into the base branch"; several tickets run in parallel, and the integration renumbers migrations if another ticket took the number (ADR 0007 rule 4). |
| M1-20/10 | 2026-10-12 | The method of `kernel/idempotency.Store` that claims a key is `Claim`, not `Begin` as in the brief's sketch; the outgoing port of sync names its insert `Insert`. | The gate rule `bothy-go-tx-only-in-platform-pg` (`.semgrep/`) refuses every `$X.Begin(...)` outside `platform/pg`, because a transaction begun elsewhere skips the phases; the claim begins no transaction, and a gate is not lowered for a name. |
| M1-20/11 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed `2xx` body runs through `SanitizeJSON` before `Store.Complete`: the stored body is the one the response writer sends (null members removed), and a body it would refuse (a null element, an integer beyond 2^53 - 1, not JSON) rolls the unit back and answers `500`. | Otherwise the effect would commit while the client gets `500` from the response writer of `StageRoute`, and every replay would answer `500` as well (`TestRefusedSuccess_RollsBack`). |
| M1-20/12 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed `2xx` without a check of the route's action in the check log (the test of `routeWriter.proven`, 02 9.3) rolls the unit back and is not stored; the response writer answers it with `500`. The generator checks of M1-20/8 also cover a `2XX` range response. | Red team, first round, Major 1: the effect committed and the row was stored while the client got `500`, and the retry then received the held-back body as a replay, which 06 "Idempotency" point 4 allows only for an answer the client was already allowed to get (`TestUnprovenSuccess_RollsBack`). The range case was a Minor of the same round (`TestGenerate_RejectsKeyedOperationWithNonJSONRangeResponse`). |
| M1-20/13 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: the chapter references name the English section titles (02 "Idempotency in the Unit of Work", "Lock order", "Rules for jobs", "Job catalogue", 04 "All other writes", 06 "Idempotency"), `jsoncanon_test.go` is listed in `tools/docs/language-allow.txt`, and the migration keeps `00007` with a checksum over the English comments. | The sorting example of [RFC 8785](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8785) section 3.2.3 (retrieved 2026-10-13) has a key with U+00F6, which the language gate refuses even escaped; the test keeps the published vector byte for byte. |
| M1-20/14 | 2026-10-14 | `sonar.coverage.exclusions` also leaves the test helper packages of the platform layer, `services/server/internal/platform/*/*test/` (`httpservertest`, `pgtest`, `jobstest`), out of the coverage measure, as M3-01/20 does for the test helpers of the modules; `sync/app` gets unit tests for the idempotency store and the cleanup. | The port lands after M3-01, and the analysis counted the new harness code of `httpservertest` as uncovered new code, although only tests of other packages call it; the store and the cleanup were covered only through the tests of other packages. |

## M1-29 Rights changes, access.changed and invariants I1 and SR8

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-29/1 | 2026-10-12 | `access.changed` is written only for an affected principal whose difference is non-empty; a change without visible effect still raises the rights version. | Ruling of the brief: later events carry the version in the envelope; an empty event would only make clients reload. |
| M1-29/2 | 2026-10-12 | I1 is checked for all active personal agents on every rights change. | Ruling of the brief: 10 section 5.1 names the minimum per action; the superset cannot miss a case, and 03 section 6.4 already asks the check for shrinking changes. |
| M1-29/3 | 2026-10-12 | The declaration of the brief is `txn.RightsChangeSpec` with the option `txn.DeclareRightsChange`; action and resource kinds are strings and `txn.Ref` names resources and targets. `txn.RightsChange(principals...)` stays and adds up with it; `Options.Covers` also requires the targets and the action of a nested unit. `RightsChangeOf` and `InRightsChange` read the declaration. | `RightsChange` is already the name of the option, and kernel ports never import each other (go-arch-lint: `kernel/txn` may not import `kernel/authorize`). |
| M1-29/4 | 2026-10-12 | The hooks of phase 1 and 3.1 are the method values `Begin` and `BeforeCommit` of `authzapp.RightsHook`, which also implements `authz.RightsChanges.AddAffected` and `VerifyInvariants`. The appender of sync and the recorder of audit are bound after the unit of work exists (`Bind`, start step `authz`); an unbound hook fails every rights change with kind internal. | The recorder writes through the unit of work whose registry holds the hook, so it cannot exist when the registry is sealed; failing closed keeps a rights change from committing without its event and audit. |
| M1-29/5 | 2026-10-12 | The deny entry of a violation goes to `Recorder.RecordDenial`, which aggregates per `audit.deny_aggregate_window` and writes in its own transaction after the rollback. `last_owner` wins over `i1_violation` when both hold. | Brief "Deny audit entry"; without an owner no personal agent can be in order either, and AZ-M26 expects `last_owner`. |
| M1-29/6 | 2026-10-12 | The view of a principal (the input of `access.changed`) counts a person as in a session: the owner role takes effect for an active owner. dm channels are not part of it; a space is visible with read on it or on one of its channels. The input type of `DiffVisible` is `domain.AccessView`. | The view is what the person sees in the app; rights changes never change dm participation; `domain.Snapshot` is the rights snapshot of M1-28 (merged names win). |
| M1-29/7 | 2026-10-12 | For declared targets authz derives the affected principals from the grants in effect on the instance, the target, its space and, for a space, its channels, with group members, the active owners and the effective operators of the channels. Fixed thread operators are not added. | The difference step drops everyone without a change, so the superset is safe; the view of an agent on a channel never depends on a thread it operates. |
| M1-29/8 | 2026-10-12 | `StructureSource.ServedTargets` returns `authz.ChannelRef{ChannelID, SpaceID}`, with a zero `ChannelID` for a space whose default operator is the agent; `StructureSource.SpaceArchived` and `ChannelFacts.Archived` (channel or space archived) are added. `Effective` caps every level at read on an archived channel, owners included, and `Decide` answers a requirement above read there with `channel_archived` (07 section 4.5 point 1). chat fills them from M1-52 to M1-54. | 03 section 6.4 point 3 needs spaces without a channel; test 10a of the brief caps a channel at read through the target path, and the cap is rights logic, which lives only in authz (B7). `grant.create` must refuse archived spaces. |
| M1-29/9 | 2026-10-12 | `AgentPolicySource.PersonalAgents` returns `authz.PersonalAgent{AgentID, CredentialHolderID}`. | `authz.AgentPolicy` already exists (M1-28) with other fields. |
| M1-29/10 | 2026-10-12 | The store refuses writes of memberships and grants and `SoftDeleteGroup` outside a unit declared as a rights change; `InsertGroup` and the new `UpdateGroup` are no rights change. New store reads: `ActiveTriggerGrants`, `ActiveGrantsOnTargets`, `ActiveMembershipsOfGroups`, `MembershipsOfUserInGroup`. | The store doc of M1-27 left the enforcement to M1-29; `group.create` and `group.update` have "Permissions" `no` in 05. |
| M1-29/11 | 2026-10-12 | Inside a unit declared as a rights change the Authorizer neither reads nor fills the snapshot cache, the version memo or the scope memo. | The unit sees its own uncommitted version; an entry cached under it would survive a rollback and could serve another change that commits the same number. |
| M1-29/12 | 2026-10-12 | `grant.revoke` resolves a revoked grant like the stored grant (visibility and the delegation guard decide); every other action still finds no revoked grant. | 06 section 2 "No effect": revoking a revoked grant succeeds without change, which `not_found` would contradict. |
| M1-29/13 | 2026-10-12 | Admin calls that may be without effect (`DeleteGroup`, `RemoveGroupMember`, `RevokeGrant`) decide that in a first unit without rights change and run the change in a second unit declared as one, which checks everything again under the lock. When that second unit finds the call without effect after all (a concurrent call did the change first), it rolls back, so no rights version is raised, and the first unit runs again to record the call without effect. An `UpdateGroup` without change audits `no_change: true` too. | No rights version for a call without effect (R-API-27), while the change itself still runs fully under the lock of `permission_version`. |
| M1-29/14 | 2026-10-12 | `AddGroupMember` closes a membership that is not removed but expired (removed_at, removed_by) before inserting the new one; a membership in effect is `409 conflict`. Claim groups: rename, add and remove are `409 conflict` with reason `claim_managed_group`; the description may change. | The unique index allows one row without `removed_at` per person and group; 07 section 2.6 names the reason for claim groups. |
| M1-29/15 | 2026-10-12 | `CreateGrant` answers a host target with `404` until M2-39 brings hosts; an archived space or channel is `409 conflict` with reason `channel_archived`, checked after `authorize` so an invisible target stays `404`. An optional revoke or delete reason is checked with SEC-IN-4 and audited as `ReasonText`. | No host can exist before M2-39; the order keeps 02 section 9.2. |
| M1-29/16 | 2026-10-12 | `AdminService` takes the caller as `actor.Actor` and puts it into the context for the recorder and the events; a different principal already in the context is an internal error. Group text limits come through `AdminDeps.Limits`; the keys `group.name_max_chars` and `group.description_max_chars` are bound when M1-48 wires the service with its routes. | The recorder reads the actor from the context only (M1-24); the brief adds no configuration key. |
| M1-29/17 | 2026-10-12 | `errs.ProblemExtender` members are merged by the problem writer in name order after the standard members; a standard name (also an absent one) is never taken, nil members and nested nulls are dropped, an internal error carries none. | 10 section 5.4 and ADR 0053. |
| M1-29/18 | 2026-10-12 | The integration tests use PostgreSQL 18 with the real identity PrincipalSource, the real unit of work, sync and audit, and the fakes of structure, agent policy and credentials. ## M1-30 Expiry of grants and memberships

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-30/1 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave7` at `8e30539`, branch `feat/M1-30-authz-expiry` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-30/2 | 2026-10-12 | No migration: `00005_authz_groups_grants.sql` (M1-27) already has `expiry_reminded_at` and `expiry_reported_at` on both tables, both `(expires_at)` indexes and the trigger that sets either grant column once. Only new sqlc queries (`queries/expiry.sql`). | Scope 1 of the brief: add only what the merged migration lacks. |
| M1-30/3 | 2026-10-12 | The jobs are `app.ExpiryService` (`Sweep`, `Remind`) in `app/expiry.go`; their arguments, handlers and `Register` live in the new package `adapters/jobs`, which `internal/wire` calls from `NewJobRegistry`. The selections and marks are the separate port `app.ExpiryStore`, implemented by the PostgreSQL repository and also needed by the rights hook (`RightsHookDeps.Expiry`); the in-memory fake store does not implement it. | `app` may not import `internal/platform` (only adapters may); `sync/adapters/jobs` is the same pattern. A separate port keeps the existing `Store` and its fake unchanged. |
| M1-30/4 | 2026-10-12 | The reporting of an expiry (selection, marker, `grant.changed` or `group.changed`, the audit entry, the views before) runs in the rights begin hook right after `permission_version` is locked, with the time of the change; the events wait for phase 3.1, where the appender accepts them. A sweep batch passes its kind and limit through the context and its work phase only reads `RightsHook.Folded`. The deny entry of a violation in a sweep batch names the resource kind (`grant` or `group`) without id, because a batch has many rows. | One clock and one lock for the selection and both views; the appender refuses events in phase 1. |
| M1-30/5 | 2026-10-12 | Every rights change folds the due, unreported expiries in before anything else (M1-30/4), not only the sweep: they are reported with event, audit entry and marker by the first unit after the expiry, and its `access.changed` carries the action of that unit. The views treat rows not due yet (and in a sweep batch the rows of a later batch) as not expired (`domain.AssumeValid.Unreported`). The brief's `AddAffectedAssumingValid` is not built: the fold records the views before itself. Outside the sweep the fold is not bounded by `authz.expiry_sweep_batch_max`. | A view must be what the client was told before the change and what `authorize` decides after it. With the expiry left to the sweep, three red team rounds reproduced lost losses and a reported gain that `authorize` denies (a membership and its group grant expiring together, a change between an expiry and the sweep, a new grant to a group of an expired member). The sweep runs every `authz.expiry_sweep_interval`, so a change folds the rows of at most one interval unless the worker was down. |
| M1-30/6 | 2026-10-12 | An invariant violation in a batch is logged as ERROR `expiry batch rejected by an invariant` with `bothy.action` and `error.type` (`i1_violation` or `last_owner`); the agent ids are in the deny audit entry the rights hook records. | The log handlers drop every attribute outside the signals catalogue, which has no agent id; the audit entry is the durable record. |
| M1-30/7 | 2026-10-12 | The reminder excludes grants of deleted groups and memberships of deleted groups in the selection itself. | A row skipped after the selection would never be marked and would fill every batch again. |
| M1-30/8 | 2026-10-12 | `access.expiring` carries `target` (kind, id, level, capabilities) only for space and channel grants; it is absent for the instance and for hosts. | Brief Scope 4.3 and 07 section 9.4. The schema `AccessTargetKind` also knows `host` for `access.expiring`; host grants exist from M2-39, whose ticket decides whether a host reminder names its target. |
| M1-30/9 | 2026-10-12 | `group.changed` carries the group with `member_count` = the memberships in effect at the time of the change, counted in phase 3.1 after the use case wrote its own memberships. | `Group.member_count` is required; counting at commit keeps it right when a change that adds or removes members also folds an expiry of the same group. |
| M1-30/10 | 2026-10-12 | `internal/wire` builds the service with the system clock. | A sweep batch uses the clock of the rights hook (M1-30/4); the reminder reads its own once per batch. |
| M1-30/11 | 2026-10-12 | `authz.Register` now declares `grant.changed`, `group.changed` and `access.expiring` next to `access.changed`. | authz appends them from this ticket on; the registration check rejects a second module for them. |
| M1-30/12 | 2026-10-12 | Configuration: `authz.expiry_sweep_interval` (duration, default `1m`, at least `1s`), `authz.expiry_reminder_lead` (duration, `48h`, at least `1s`) and `authz.expiry_sweep_batch_max` (int, `500`, 1 to 2147483647, the range of the SQL limit parameter). | 07 section 14 gives the defaults; the bounds keep every value usable. |
| M1-30/13 | 2026-10-12 | Recipients of `access.expiring` follow 07 section 9.4 and the brief: for a grant to a group all active owners and `granted_by`. The note of `access.expiring` in 05 section 6 (generated from `actions.yaml`: "to the subject (for groups, the person)") is not changed here. | The brief rules this recipient set; the catalogue note is outside the files this ticket may change and goes to the specification lane. |
| M1-30/14 | 2026-10-12 | Both jobs are registered in the one job registry every role builds (as `sync.partition_maintenance` and `sync.idempotency_cleanup`); only `worker` and `all` run handlers, because only they start River (M1-14). The registration tests check queue, period and arguments, not the role. | An `api` process must accept exactly the kinds a worker runs; the role split is the job runtime's and is tested there. |
| M1-30/15 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (NEIN, one reproduced major): fixed through `AssumeValid.Unreported` (M1-30/5) with `TestExpirySweep_MembershipAndGroupGrantTogether` and `TestExpirySweep_TwoGrantsOnOneTargetInTwoBatches`. Minors fixed: the host target follows 07 (M1-30/8), the active owners are read once per reminder batch, and adapter tests cover the membership selections and marks. Minors kept: the invariant log without agent ids (M1-30/6); `grant.changed` of a channel target carries no `space_id` while `internal/wire` passes the empty structure source (until M1-53, as for the rights hook of M1-29). | Findings of the red team fixed in the same ticket where they are in scope. |
| M1-30/16 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 2 (NEIN, one reproduced major of the same class: a revocation or removal between an expiry and the sweep reported nothing, and a new grant in that window reported an `added` the client already had): fixed in one place, the views of the rights hook (M1-30/5), with the regression tests `TestExpiryWindow_*`. | The second finding of the same class: fixed for the class, not the instance. |
| M1-30/17 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 3 (NEIN, one reproduced major: a new grant to a group whose membership had expired unreported told the expired member `added` for a target `authorize` denies): fixed for the class by folding due expiries into every rights change (M1-30/4, /5), with `TestExpiryWindow_NoPhantomGain`, `TestExpiryWindow_GroupDeleteAndReAdd` and the changed `TestExpiryWindow_NewGrantAfterExpiry`. `TestExpirySweep_ExactlyOnce` makes its failing run with a vetoing subscriber of `authorize.AccessChanged`, because the sweep's events now go through the appender of the rights hook. | Third finding in the same area: the mechanism now has one rule (report a due expiry before computing any view) instead of assumptions in the views. |

Test `TestExpiryWindow_FoldAuditIsSystem`. Minor fixed: `member_count` (M1-30/9). Minor kept: a denied admin request folds before its use case authorizes and then rolls the fold back; correct, only work. | System actions never pretend a human did them (05 rule 1.10, R-AZ-15); the brief asks the audit row without actor. |
